# Open research questions

Living list. Every entry needs an experiment or RE dig before implementation
may treat it as fact.

**Many of these can be answered by playing the game.**
`observation-checklist.md` collects the ones a person in front of DK2 can settle
by looking, as concrete experiments, and says which ones playing cannot settle.
Check it before writing anything up as UNKNOWN.

Conventions. Rows are never renumbered, reordered or deleted, so file order is
not numeric order — see "Numbering" at the end. The Status cell opens with the
state (OPEN, UNKNOWN, HYPOTHESIS, ANSWERED (source, date), BUILT (ADR-nnnn),
WITHDRAWN, SUPERSEDED, CUT) and carries the answer, its confidence tag and a
pointer to the note, ADR or test that holds the evidence. The Blocking cell says
what the question stops, and names a live divergence from DK2 where one is
known. A shipped variable recorded as CUT has no reader in `DKII.EXE`; one
recorded as DISABLED has a reader and ships as zero (ADR-0085).

Allocating a number: take the one after the highest in this file. **Q121 is a
hole** — never allocated, never to be reused. **Q39 labels two rows**, both
kept; Q37a, Q37b, Q55x and Q118c are separate suffixed ids rather than
duplicates, and none of the four is a mistake to fix.

| # | Question | Status | Blocking |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q602 | How does the ordinary camera produce the terrain visibility polygon? | ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-15), BUILT: nine transformed camera-volume points are clipped by every pair against native heights[-9,0], deduplicated horizontally, angularly wrapped and converted with biased float-bit rounding. Signed16.16 scan conversion includes bothX endpoints and excludes maximumY. `ClassicLightVisibility` passes12 targeted cases. See [classic-light-visibility.md](classic-light-visibility.md). | Automatic publication can consume actual pose, lens/viewport and native view distance; rendered original parity remains a visual gate. |
| Q597 | How does each mesh draw gather nearby tile-cache lights? | ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-15), bounded gather BUILT (ADR-0487): `0x00576230` selects up to12 static and24 total sources from a rolling7x7 window with strict horizontal bounds/ties and native stamp invalidation; no local LOS query. `0x00572CF0` supplies the shared table to primitive preparation. Evidence: [classic-lighting.md section9](classic-lighting.md#9-per-tile-terrain-point-lights). | Automatic native source publication still needs cache visibility/preparation (Q602); unrestricted authored-tile sums are not equivalent. |
| Q1 | Simulation ticks-per-second of DK2 v1.70? | **ANSWERED (binary + owner, 2026-09-07), BUILT (ADR-0089): a game second is 4 ticks of 250 ms.** All 66 shipped levels carry 4 in the LEVEL field OpenKeeper calls `ticksPerSec`, which the loader copies into variable 19 (`0x006F2590`), the seconds-to-ticks multiplier of Q132; the creature per-tick method, the free-imp deadline and the job re-roll gate all read it as one game second, and the owner's portal stopwatch (Q86) pins one game second to one real second. STRONGLY INFERRED from the binary, measured at 3.93–3.95 ticks/s live. `SimulationTiming.TicksPerSecond` = 4. Evidence: `balance-variables-in-the-binary.md`, ADR-0089. `GameTicks` 340 is variable 74, not a rate (Q83) | — |
| Q2 | Creature job-selection priority order? OpenKeeper uses lair→sleep→eat→work — theirs or DK2's? | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-05), BUILT (ADR-0082): neither — it is not a priority list.** Per think DK2 runs the current state's update, then hunger, then sleep, then the mood's job chooser, which builds cumulative ranges from `HappyJobs` / `UnhappyJobs` / `AngryJobs`, rolls `rand(100)` and then falls through the list in order; a re-roll waits 5 × variable 19 = 20 ticks (Q1). Unhappy and angry choosers try for a lair first; heroes, neutrals and workers (Q24) have their own routines. STRONGLY INFERRED. Evidence: `job-selection.md`, `lair-acquisition.md` §5 and §8. Built: `JobRules`, `CreatureJobSystem`, `CreatureJobTests` | — |
| Q3 | What RNG does DK2 use, and does its sequence matter observably? | ANSWERED: a 32-bit LCG (`×0x0019660D + 0x3C6EF35F`, rotate right 13, mod n) on one world stream seeded 1 at level start; built as `Dk2Lcg`, not the default (ADR-0275). Still open: the purpose of 2 of the 15 C-runtime `rand()` callers | `research/dk2-random.md` |
| Q4 | What does the map's 11-byte sight-table entry encode? | **PARTLY ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-14).** It is a sight cache, separate from the 16-byte tile. The 225 entries encode centre bearing, corner slot endpoints and signed offsets; their sort key is squared tile distance. Entries 1..77 drive angular occlusion. **Open:** validate exact endpoint formulas and equal-key order in disassembly, then compare boundary/corner visibility in the original. Evidence: `fog-reveal-sources.md` §3.1. | M3 terrain runtime |
| Q5 | `Follow_Path` (36 bytes) contents; pathfinding algorithm | **ANSWERED and BUILT (binary, ADR-0155/ADR-0157, wired 2026-09-17; `navigation-and-steering.md`, `navigation-mesh-and-search.md`, `steering-behaviours.md`).** DK2 routes over a **triangulated navigation mesh** with a greedy two-ended search, a funnel and a gate re-walk (`DK2.Pathfinding`), and moves by **four weighted steering behaviours** integrated by Euler (`DK2.Simulation.Steering`), joined to the world by `WorldSteering` and `CreatureMovementSystem`. `Follow_Path` is a **stateless global singleton**; the per-creature state is the steering body at creature `+0x124` | The grid search survives only behind `TryFindNearest`, which chooses work by walking distance (ADR-0157) |
| Q6 | `f3AF_workModifier` vs `f3B3_workEnhancement` semantics | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-05), BUILT (ADR-0062).** `creature[0x3AF]` is the **base work rate**, set to `0xCCC` = 0.8 by the constructor and shown on the status bar as *"Efficiency: 80%"*; `creature[0x3B3]` is a **temporary bonus**, zero at spawn, where the `IncreasedWorkRate*` percentages land (slapping 50, casino big win 35, tortured types 25 — but see Q81 and ADR-0090: those three are read by the research adder, not written to `0x3B3`). An application is `tabledDelta × (0x3AF + 0x3B3)` once a tick. `TileWorkRules` applies the 0.8; `SlapWorkFasterDurationSeconds` drives the bonus. `tile-work.md`, "The work rate in the binary" | — |
| Q7 | Creature mood/anger model | **PARTLY ANSWERED, BUILT (ADR-0050, ADR-0053, ADR-0081, ADR-0087; `creature-anger.md`).** Owner (2026-08-29), in order: (1) no lair, (2) hunger, (3) not being paid, (4) the company of hated creatures, converted heroes named. The record carries `AngerNoLair`, `AngerNoFood`, `AngerNoPay`, `AngerNoWork`, `AngerSlap`, `AngerInHand`; the fourth cause has no field. Ceiling 32767 (Q106, Q114), linear accrual, unhappy at `UnhappyThreshold` 50 %; mood is the largest of six counters, not the sum (Q202); `CreatureFlags2.NeverAngered` (Skeleton, Jack) switches it off (Q210). Still UNKNOWN: the hated-company input and the decay | M8 |
| Q8 | Combat resolution formula (damage, armour, hit timing) | **BUILT for melee (ADR-0163–0166): a creature acquires (`combat-acquisition.md`), walks up, winds up and lands `MeleeDamageRules` blows through `CreatureHealthRules.Apply`, hearts through `DamageHeart` (`combat-fight-state.md`), fears, backs off and runs (`combat-fear.md`); a worker fights at its heart, withdraws from a hero or raises the alarm (`combat-alarm.md`).** Combat is creature state 199/200, whose handler `spFighting` starts action 17 (melee) or 16 (spell). Read and not built: state 8's walk and the victory pause (`combat-approach-and-victory.md`, Q368/Q369/Q376). Left: spells, resistance (Q335) | M7. Heroes, hero parties, the Combat Pit and Call to Arms wait on the rest |
| Q9 | Payday procedure & timing (`GetPaid`, `SkipNextPayment`) | **ANSWERED (owner 2026-08-29/30; binary 2026-09-06), BUILT (ADR-0078).** An unpaid creature gets unhappy, then angry, then leaves; a converted one rebels. `PayDayFrequencySeconds` and `TimeBeforeFirstPayDaySeconds` are both 600. The clock credits each creature `Pay` scaled by level with `+=`, so a missed payday compounds, then sends it to the nearest Treasury or gold-holding Dungeon Heart; two failed collections and it stops. `GetPaid` (`+0x3E2`) skips one payday, spent whether needed or not; `SkipNextPayment` (`+0x3EA` bit 5) skips one collection (ADR-0217). `payday.md`, `PaydaySystem` | `PayDayCutOffTimeSeconds` (payday.md §2.1) not built. Nothing in that path charges `AngerNoPay` (Q7) |
| Q10 | GOG exe = v1.70 byte-for-byte? MD5 `2f3bbc21eae8c3aa468136baa303036d` (DKII.EXE, 2,987,568 bytes) recorded; verify against Flame's expectations before memory-probing | STRONGLY INFERRED (GOG ships 1.7) | M5 oracle work |
| Q11 | Units/meaning of `moodChange` and `manaChange` on JobPreference records | **PARTLY ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-05).** Signed 16-bit; the loader `0x00492320` divides each by variable 19 into per-tick values (so per-second rates, STRONGLY INFERRED) in 6-byte entries at `def+0x628`/`+0x63C`/`+0x64A`. No reader found: the list accessors `0x004944D0`/`0x00494550`/`0x004945D0` and all eight callers use only chance and job. Ours reads them signed (ADR-0375). **Open:** a consumer outside the scanned offsets. Evidence: `kwd-creatures.md` | — |
| Q12 | Is 32767 a general "never" sentinel for hungerRate/timeAwake, or Imp-specific? | **ANSWERED (data; `creature-needs.md` "32767 means never, per need"): a per-need sentinel.** Eight creatures never eat, six never sleep, neither set contains the other (Vampire sleeps and never eats; Dwarf and Stone Knight eat and never sleep). The binary gates the lair attempt on `TimeSleep ≠ 0` (Q104). The Imp (owner, 2026-08-29: imps never eat) is one instance | — |
| Q13 | `expForNextLevel` is uniform 3000 (2800 uniques) — does per-level progression actually come from VARIABLES CreatureStats rows (StatType 13)? | STRONGLY INFERRED. Stat 13 `EXPERIENCE_POINTS_FOR_NEXT_LEVEL` scales 130 % to 1000 % across the 9-level table (Q119); the adder's rules are Q127 (ADR-0083) | — |
| Q14 | The 88 extra bytes in the embedded 5537-byte CREATURES record variant | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-05).** Never read. The chunk reader `0x0056A250` zero-fills a 5449-byte record and reads the record into it; `0x00568E30` reads nothing past file `0x1548` and `0x00492320` nothing past staging `0x1935`. No 5537 constant exists in the binary. Built as ADR-0375: any size, zero-filled to 5449. Evidence: `kwd-creatures.md` | — |
| Q15 | Purpose of the THINGS(190) chunk inside GlobalVariables.kwd (39 items) | **PARSED (ADR-0091, `kwd-record-fields.md` §THINGS).** THINGS payloads decode by tag: 194 Object, 195 Trap, 196 Door, 197 ActionPoint, 198–200 creatures, 201 HeroParty, 202 DeadBody (layout a HYPOTHESIS, Q221), 203 EffectGenerator, 204 Room, 205 Camera; 6,318 shipped records. What the global file's 39 items are *for* is not recorded | — |
| Q16 | MAP tile bytes 3 (`flags`, bridge-related) and 4 — meaning? | **Byte 3 ANSWERED (2026-09-03, ADR-0031): a bridge over lava** — 1 everywhere in all 66 shipped maps, 2 on bridge tiles over lava and nowhere else, no third value (Q64, `BridgeRules`). **Byte 4 CONFIRMED unused** by the 2013 KeeperKlan reversal thread (`external-resources.md`): `{BYTE idTerrain; BYTE idPlayer; BYTE flags; BYTE unused;}`, "only a '2' bit here is interpreted to do anything special at load". Our sweep: byte 4 is uniform across a level (Level6b all 1, Level20 all 0), uncorrelated with water or lava — a level property, not tile data | — |
| Q17 | MAP `playerId` numbering: which id is neutral? (level1: p1=32, p2=1048, p3=60) | **ANSWERED (2026-09-04, ADR-0043, `PlayerIds`): 2 is neutral**, 0 unowned terrain, 1 the hero side, 3–7 keepers. Three confirmations: OpenKeeper's `Player` constants; every dungeon-heart tile, creature pool and availability entry across 66 levels belongs to a player in 3–7; level 1's p2=1048 is its unclaimed portal and player-2 walls, p3=60 its keeper's tiles. Closing it fixed a four-month defect (Q95, ADR-0045): a stale owner byte was never cleared because "which id means nobody" was open | — |
| Q18 | `maxHealthTypeTerrainId` on Claimed Path points at Rock — does not fit the "upgrade at full health" reading. Second meaning for path tiles? | **ANSWERED (2026-08-29, `tile-work.md`): no second meaning.** The field is the destination of the terrain's *upward job*, triggered by the work, not by health level. `FillInable` falls on exactly the five floor types, and six terrain types ship at full health pointing elsewhere, which refutes the level-triggered reading. Claimed Path's transition to Rock is inert: no mechanism triggers it (Q219) | — |
| Q19 | How often are the VARIABLES health deltas applied (per tick? per second?) — needed to turn dig/claim rates into real timings | **ANSWERED (2026-08-30, refined 2026-09-05).** Deltas are per **game** second; a worker applies `delta × efficiency / ticksPerGameSecond` once a tick, so a default worker at 80 % does four fifths of the tabled delta in a game second. Established from the owner's 273 gold a swing (Q47). The per-application amount and the application count per job are what the simulation depends on | — |
| Q20 | Does `DigRockHealth` −5000 (one strike against Rock's 165) match the original? | **ANSWERED — yes, as shipped** (full-state captures, 2026-09-28): Rock goes in one strike. The −500 override (ADR-0023) came from an animation count and is retired (ADR-0280). `tile-work.md` §Q20 | — |
| Q21 | Does a gold seam release its 3000 gold in proportion to the health removed, or in a lump when it collapses? | **ANSWERED — gradually, as it is mined** (owner, 2026-08-29). Implemented proportionally | — |
| Q22 | `FillInHealth` is 5, but every fill-inable tile is already at maximum health. How long does filling one in take? | **WITHDRAWN (2026-09-08):** the question presupposed a fill-in action DK2 does not have (Q219), and `FillInHealth` has no reader | — |
| Q46 | **How long does claiming a tile take?** | **MEASURED (owner, 2026-08-29): 2–4 jumps, about 2 seconds.** `ClaimTileHealth` 825 gives 165 an application against Dirt Path's 660 — four applications, one jump each, 1.0 s of work at four a second; the rest is the walk on and the post-claim linger (Q49). Claiming is not a marked job: an imp takes it automatically when unowned floor touches the keeper's own by an edge (`JobPriority.Claim`, `ClaimFreshlyDug`), so "mark a tile for claiming" is impossible. The visual damage-stage reading of 2026-09-07 was withdrawn on the owner's word — the stage mechanism is real but predicts appearance steps, not swings (`tile-work.md`) | — |
| Q47 | **How much does one swing do?** | **ANSWERED (owner 2026-08-30; binary 2026-09-05/07), BUILT (ADR-0020, corrected by ADR-0089): 273 gold a swing = 60 health**, a fifth of `MineGoldHealth`'s −300, so a seam is 11 swings. The fifth is Efficiency spread over four ticks, not a cadence: a work handler reads the rate already divided by variable 19 and multiplies by `creature[0x3af] + creature[0x3b3]` (`0xccc` = 0.8), so an application is `tabled ÷ 4 × 0.8`, once a tick, four a second. Slap → 90 % for 25 s (Q159), imp dropped beside its marked tile → 90 %, both → 100 %; measured 272–273, 309–310, 341. ADR-0133 supersedes the detail: the shift floors, so 825 gives 164; ADR-0387 the rounding order (`tile-work.md`). `tile-work.md` | — |
| Q23 | Is a dig marker per player or global? Assumed per player. | STRONGLY INFERRED | — |
| Q24 | **Worker job priority.** In what order does an imp prefer its jobs, and what makes it start an unprompted one? | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-08-29), BUILT (ADR-0018, refined by ADR-0058): marked excavation first — rock or gold alike — then claiming, then reinforcing.** After that an imp runs around the dungeon and interacts with the environment and creatures (not implemented, Q41). Carrying gold is the tail of a mining job, not a chosen job (Q31); fetching floor gold sits in the positional tier (Q146, Q147). `imp-tasks.md`, `imp-task-priority.md` | — |
| Q25 | Of several marked tiles, which does an imp take? | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-29):** the nearest by straight-line distance to the tile's centre, walls before seams on a tie, seams measured from the last seam after a mining job (`0x00469AF0`). The owner's "closest by walking distance" is the same answer in open ground. BUILT (ADR-0282). `imp-task-priority.md` §2.2 | — |
| Q26 | How many workers can take one tile job? | **ANSWERED, BUILT (owner and binary, 2026-09-27):** digging and mining allow three per accessible face; reinforcing allows one per face (at most four around a wall); claiming allows one on its tile. See `imp-tasks.md`, ADR-0026 and ADR-0030. A surplus worker searches elsewhere. | Task stealing by a nearer imp remains unbuilt (Q155). |
| Q27 | Can a tile be worked from a diagonal neighbour? | **ANSWERED (owner): orthogonal only.** The observed digging limit, three imps on each of four faces, gives twelve on a block. See `imp-tasks.md`. | Closed. |
| Q28 | Is DK2's creature movement four- or eight-connected — specifically, can a creature pass diagonally between two solid tiles? | **ANSWERED, BUILT (ADR-0025, amended ADR-0339): eight-connected**, octile costs (Q48), Dijkstra nearest (Q25). **A corner-only gap is no route**: the region fill `0x004C91A0` is edge-only; six Level 2 imps (3063-3082) passed a wall behind one | **Split by the binary (2026-09-17):** DK2's routing refuses a corner-only pair before any search (`0x004C9400`); its mover commits a step by the tile it lands in, never testing the two it passes between, so a *steered* crossing is not forbidden. Which the owner saw is open; Levels 7 and 10 captured no such crossing in 258,536 steps, but one corner-only pair saw traffic (steering-behaviours.md §8) |

Stat 21 `SPEED_TILES_PER_SECOND` in the level table carries the unit in its name too (Q118c) | — |
| Q30 | Does terrain weight a path, or only the mover's speed? | **CONFIRMED (binary, rechecked 2026-10-10):** greedy search scales its squared-distance key ×4 in water and ×64 in lava; it does not accumulate travel time. Current routing matches. The owner observed a shorter water crossing chosen over a dry route on 2026-08-29; that layout does not establish universal shortest-path selection. Water speed is Q118. See navigation-mesh-and-search.md §3–§4 | Exact original-versus-modern bridge choices in the reported Snapdragon layout remain unmeasured |
| Q48 | What does a **diagonal step cost** an eight-connected mover (Q28)? | **(a) ANSWERED, BUILT, CONFIRMED (ADR-0025).** Speed is in tiles per second (Q29), so a diagonal step covers √2 tile-widths and takes 1.41× as long; the owner (2026-09-03) saw a creature glide along the diagonal rather than staircase. `GridPathfinder.DiagonalCost` 14 against `StraightCost` 10, in search and mover alike. **(b) What the pathfinder charges when choosing between routes is OPEN** and probably unobservable: the two cost models diverge only where a route with fewer steps is longer in true distance | — |
| Q31 | **Where does mined gold go?** | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-08-29), BUILT.** With free treasury or heart capacity the mining imp carries gold up to `maxGoldHeld`, runs to the drop spot and stores it; with nowhere to put it, it keeps mining and a lump accumulates on the floor in front of it. Carrying is the tail of a mining job (Q24). Built in `CreatureState.GoldHeld` + `ImpTaskSystem`; gold the imp cannot hold is reported as `gold_uncollected` and dropped, not left as a pile (Q138). Selling a treasury tile drops its gold as a pile (owner, 2026-09-03; ADR-0029, ADR-0055). See Q50 (when the imp stops mining), Q65 (`MaxGoldPileOutsideTreasury` is not a pile limit) | — |
| Q32 | Is `RoomDef.Cost` per tile or per room? | **ANSWERED — per tile, the record's number is the price** (owner, 2026-08-30: Treasury 200, Lair 300, Hatchery 300, Library 600, Training Room 500, all matching ROOMS). `RoomsOnRealDataTests.ARoomCostsWhatTheOriginalChargesForIt_PerTile` | — |
| Q33 | Must a new room tile touch land the keeper already holds? | **ANSWERED — yes** (owner, 2026-08-29); the binary agrees for water and lava (`0x0044F630`), and land needs ownership. BUILT for every player 2026-10-01 (ADR-0304, `bridge-placement.md`) | — |
| Q34 | What does selling a room refund? | **ANSWERED, BUILT (owner 2026-08-29/2026-09-03; ADR-0029): 50 % of cost, per tile.** `RoomSellValuePercentageOfCost` is **50** (this row once said 100 — a transcription error; `game-database-dump.md` had 50). `RoomDef.ReturnPercentage` is a per-room gate: 100 for every buildable room, 0 for the hero gates. Whether they multiply or override is indistinguishable on shipped data. `DoorSellValuePercentageOfCost` and `TrapSellValuePercentageOfCost` are 50. Built as `RoomRules.RefundPerTile` + `RoomSellSystem`; the tile reverts to claimed floor and the keeper keeps the ground (CONFIRMED). Gold on a sold treasury tile drops as a pile (Q31, ADR-0055). Selling a bridge — Q64 | — |
| Q35 | Which treasury tile does a deposit land in, and which does a withdrawal drain? | **PARTLY ANSWERED (2026-09-05), deposit half:** the carrier keeps the nearest reachable Treasury or Heart below capacity (squared distance to the room anchor, reachability first; a heart is skipped when one unidentified flag bit is set), then walks that room's tile chain and takes the first tile below `MaxGoldPerTreasuryTile` — first free along the chain (`lair-acquisition.md` §4), not nearest. Three failure messages: no room, all full, none reachable. Unchecked oddity: the walk starts from the anchor of the *last* capacity-passing room examined. Ours: nearest tile (`rooms-and-gold.md`) | The withdrawal order, and the lump beside a full treasury (Q140), are OPEN; totals are unaffected either way |
| Q36 | Do a room's `RecommendedSizeX/Y` and `TileConstruction` constrain what a keeper may build, or only how it is drawn? | **ANSWERED — they do not constrain building** (owner, 2026-08-29): a 1x1 temple is buildable. What the sizes relate to is function — Q45 | — |

| Q37b | **Is a claimed mana vault worth +100 a second?** | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-08-30), CONFIRMED:** *"each claimed mana vault raises mana production by +100"*, and an unclaimed one pays nothing. `ManaRules.GainPerSecond` reads `TerrainDef.ManaGain` off the tile's terrain — Claimed Vault 100, Mana Vault 0. The first mana *magnitude* checked against the original rather than only its sign, and a second independent confirmation that the unit is per second (Q39) | — |

| Q38 | Is `MaximumManaGainPerSecond` (500) a per-keeper cap or one shared across the level? | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-08-29) — not shared:** one keeper's dungeon does not throttle another's mana. Implemented per keeper | — |
| Q39 | Does mana accrue smoothly or in whole seconds? | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-04): whole seconds.** `Mana_UpdatePlayer` `0x004C10E0` waits 4 turns past `player+0x9A`, then adds the whole second's net to mana `+0x82` in one step. It normally re-stamps at `0x004C16B1`; two rare exits (`0x004C13F5`, `0x004C1550`) skip that, so the next turn pays again. Ours pays once a game second (ADR-0017), which matches. `mana.md`, the ceiling section | Nothing depends on it |
| Q40 | **Losing your dungeon heart is defeat.** What does defeat do? | **ANSWERED and BUILT (ADR-0162 as amended; `win-and-defeat.md` §7).** DK2 stamps `KilledByPlayerId` when the heart object dies and, next turn, sets `Status` 3 if heroes or neutrals struck the blow and 1 (conquered) if a keeper did, then awards 2 to the last keeper standing. The consequences are `0x004DAA10`, `DefeatRules`: the heart's gold to neutral piles, every room but the heart and the hero gates to neutral, roomless owned tiles to their destroyed terrain on the death turn; from the fifth turn workers are killed and the rest leave through state 194 (`LeavingRules`). Combat lands on the heart since ADR-0164 | Left (Q358): `0x004DAE40`'s local-player collapse and object 100, the Destructor — research only |
| Q41 | What does an imp do when it has no excavation, nothing to claim and nothing to reinforce? | **CONFIRMED (owner): it runs around and interacts with the environment and creatures**; idle imps run, stop, seem to wait, run again (2026-09-24). **Body read** (`imp-idle-and-wandering.md`, STRONGLY INFERRED): state 12 walks (state 87, an owned tile 4–7 out) or plays action 43/44 in place; each walk resumes via 1 → 10. **BUILT (ADR-0224)**, with the idle actions' length guessed | Which animations 43/44 are; what state `+0xA7` holds on re-entry |
| Q42 | Are claiming and reinforcing taken from ground the keeper already holds, or from any reachable floor? | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-08-29), BUILT (ADR-0028) — only from ground the keeper already holds.** Territory spreads outwards; it does not appear in islands. Which tile carries the condition follows from the work stance (Q49): a wall job is taken standing on the keeper's own land, so the **standing** tile must be owned; a floor job is done standing on the tile being claimed, which is by definition not owned yet, so it is the **target** that must touch owned land. Both readings say the same thing about the map | — |
| Q43 | Is the worker limit a reservation or physical crowding? | **ANSWERED, BUILT:** the limit belongs to a job and face; a full face is skipped during job search. A claim now logs `steps=2` where it logged `steps=1`. The ~500 ms linger is not implemented — Q49 | — |

A graveyard's is the rim of its **interior** — 0, 1, 4, 3, 8 for 2x2, 3x3, 4x4, 5x3, 5x5, built as `RoomInstance.InteriorRimTileCount` | Nothing. The 4x4 alone had read as "interior tile count", which the 5x5's 8 against that rule's 9 refuted; a 2x2 interior is entirely rim, so that size could never have separated them |

The Dark Angel's temple entry is `size ≥ 0`, so owner and manual stand against the v1.70 record (Q80). The first clause rules out wall jobs (`Solid` tiles); the second ruled out fill-in, which DK2 lacks (Q219). **The pause is real, NOT IMPLEMENTED** (owner, 2026-09-03): an imp that has claimed hesitates before the next tile; one whose wall has just fallen moves straight on, so the delay is claim-specific. ~500 ms on a four-application (1.0 s) claim is a ~50 % tax on territory spread. No `...Seconds` variable is a candidate; all are whole numbers | Time a chain of claims — `observation-checklist.md` |

A level 1 Imp (1,400) breaks off after **four** swings (1,090–1,092 against 1,060), so a seam is three trips of 1,090, 1,091 and 819; a level 2 one (1,470, full at 1,130) after five. Level 1's capture: imp 455 holds 1,092 from tick 216. The earlier "one more swing would not fit" hypothesis gave five swings at level 1. `functions/00465870.md`, `imp-hands-full.md` | — |

A heart is 5x5 = 25 tiles, and 25 − 16 = 9 = 3x3 — the raised centre the heart structure stands on, which a screenshot of the original shows directly, gold piles ringing it. The platform is found from the room instance's bounding-box centre, which needs nothing from `TileConstruction` (`FiveByFiveRotated`, still unread) and lets a half-destroyed heart degrade sensibly rather than by a hardcoded 16. Asserted against `level1`'s actual heart: 25 tiles, 16 storing, 16000 | — |

Implemented as `base × (imps − MinimumImpThreshold + 1)`, which needs no counter and cannot drift from the roster | — |

The 406-byte record size is confirmed: their field list totals exactly 406 and five scattered offset comments checkpoint the running total, all landing. The trap upkeep is confirmed the same way — `0x0202` is `manaUsage`, unsigned short — and their record adds a distinction the measurement could not: a 4-byte `manaCostToFire` at `0x01D4`, which nothing here reads. | Q55 | **What is in a `KeeperSpells.kwd` record?** | **PARTLY ANSWERED: two fields are parsed** (`KwdKeeperSpells`, 13 records, 406 bytes each, 5278 total) — the mana cost at `0x11C` (Possession 500 to Summon Horny 100000; twelve values, listed in `docs/DECISIONS.md`) and the per-second upkeep at `0x00E2` (Q53). | Q56 | **Does the free-imp clock run while a keeper is at full strength, or only while short?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-05), BUILT (ADR-0057) — neither: it is a DEADLINE, not a clock.** One field on the keeper means *the earliest time a free imp may be handed out*, and it is written in exactly one place: immediately after a free imp is made, as `now + TimeBeforeFreeImpGeneratedSeconds × variable 19`. Nothing else touches it — not the count recovering, not the keeper being served. So a keeper thirty seconds at full strength carries a deadline thirty seconds stale and **the first replacement after a loss is immediate**; only later ones are an interval apart. The owner filmed that (about 1.4 s, then ~5 s each). `PlayerState.NextFreeImpTick`, `imps-and-mana.md` | — |

No separate accumulator, so reinforcing Rock costs 660 − 165 = **495**, 50 applications at `ReinforceWallHealth`'s 10. `TileState.UpwardWork` is deleted: progress *is* the tile's health. **The timing is NOT confirmed.** The owner timed one wall at 13.38, 13.77 and 13.1–13.4 s against ADR-0089's **12.5 s** — below every reading | **ANSWERED (run 15, ADR-0133): the deficit was right and the rate was one too high.** 55 applications of 9, not 50 of 10 — 56 ticks, or 14.2 s at the 254 ms a tick measured |

Clean partition: 2 never occurs on the other 38 terrain types, every 2 tile borders lava, every 1 bridge tile on a hazard borders water. The behaviour is confirmed, the storage STRONGLY INFERRED. `BridgeRules.RevertsTo` is the single decider for what a removed tile leaves (`BridgesOnRealDataTests`). Answers Q16's bridge half | — |
| Q65 | **Can a floor pile hold everything a treasury tile can?** `MaxGoldPerTreasuryTile` is 3000 and `MaxGoldPileOutsideTreasury` 2000 | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-05), BUILT — and the premise was wrong: `MaxGoldPileOutsideTreasury` is not a limit on a pile.** Its global has three readers, all imp code choosing where to set a load down, all testing the same way: a spot holding a pile strictly greater than the variable is not chosen. Nothing clamps a pile to it, and two of the three pile-creating paths exceed it routinely — a sold treasury tile becomes a pile of 3000, a creature carrying 7500 leaves three of 2500. So it answers *where an imp puts gold down*, not *how big a pile may be*. `FloorGold.MaxPile` / `AcceptsMore`, never applied as a clamp. `gold-on-the-floor.md` §3 | — |

It only started mattering with ADR-0025: while movement was four-connected it was moot, and once eight-connected an imp can reach a corner-touching tile, so the two readings give visibly different dungeon shapes. This does not settle the just-dug claim's eight-neighbour mark scan, a different test in a different routine, orthogonal in the original too (Q154) | — |

Its two readers are installed in the work-handler table by `0x004950C0` and damage only ground the predicate at `0x00454000` admits. No new simulation field | `terrain-lattice.md` §9 |

Fixed: the mover re-checks each waypoint as it steps on and drops the route if blocked; the standing tile is deliberately not re-checked, so a creature on bad ground can leave. `WallsBlockMovementTests`, `DugOutTilesStayDugOutTests` | — |

So torturing type T boosts that keeper's type-T researchers and is no general work-speed bonus; DK2 computes `(variable 19 + 25)`, giving 29 % net over four per-tick contributions. **Its two siblings are wrong too, in different ways (ADR-0090):** effective rates are casino +140 % (tabled 35), tortured type +29 % (25), slap +200 % (50). | Q84 | **What are variable ids 66 and 77?** Both appear only in per-level files | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-09); 77 BUILT (ADR-0491), 66 BUILT (ADR-0546).** A level carries several records of each and our reader keeps the last, which made 66 look like a player count. **66 is an alliance** (`0x0051DC90`): players `value` and `unk1` become allies. 63 levels ally heroes with neutral and every keeper with neutral; `level18` also allies its three computer keepers (Q576). **77 overrides one field of one creature type** (`0x0051DD10`, `0x00494A70`): creature `value`, `CreatureStatId` `unk1`, new value `unk2`. `level1` gives the Lord of the Land MeleeDamage 200 and Threat 125. `variables-corpus-sweep.md` §Q84 | 66 under Q576 |
| Q85 | **What do `MiscVariable`'s `Unk1` and `Unk2` mean?** | **ANSWERED (sweep, 2026-09-07): not trailing fields.** The payload is three dwords read three ways by id â `(creatureId, value, playerId)` for `CreaturePool`, `(statId, level, value)` for `CreatureStats` â so they are slots 2 and 3 of a generic payload the structured ids fill with real data. For a plain variable they carry nothing and nothing reads them: 18 distinct pairs, `(0,0)` on 204 records, one id differing between files. By level family, not id: `Secret*`'s `(20, 5915252)` = `0x005A45B4` on eight separate maps looks like stale serialiser memory. `variables-corpus-sweep.md` | Residual: whether the walker reads slots 2–3 before dispatching; the stubs store one value, so neither option changes anything |
| Q86 | **How does a portal behave?** | **ANSWERED, BUILT (ADR-0042, ADR-0086; `portals.md`).** The pool is a standing per-type limit, not a draining quota (§2, Q87). Capacity and attraction are re-evaluated every tick, so a second portal raises the cap at once and selling your only lair stops the next goblin. The interval is per portal, clock and tally: `n² + EntranceGenerationSpeedSeconds` game seconds, `n` being that room's own count of creatures generated, never decremented, so killing one does not speed it up. Read at `0x004DBBE0`; stopwatch MAE 1.0–1.4 s (owner, 2026-09-07). Both reset on an ownership change and nothing else; a portal cannot be built or sold; five levels override the base | Open: whether a sacked creature's replacement is immediate or after the interval (Q90) |
| Q87 | **Is a level's `CreaturePool` a standing limit or a draining quota?** Level 1's pool is `creature 7 = 15` | **Two different fifteens.** Capacity is CONFIRMED (owner, 2026-09-07): 15 for the first claimed portal, +5 per portal after — `CreaturesSupportedByFirstPortal` (73), `CreaturesSupportedPerAdditionalPortal` (177). A `CreaturePool` entry is a different quantity, and **what it is a quota of is OPEN.** Implemented as a standing limit (ADR-0042) — fifteen goblins at a time — because OpenKeeper compares it against the keeper's current count, the manual says a sacked creature *"[is] replaced"*, and the file cannot record depletion. Against: the manual's *"multiplayer pools are finite"* | Late-game pacing. Experiment: claim a second portal and watch the cap go 15 → 20 |
| Q88 | **Is a portal's centre tile walkable?** | **ANSWERED, CONFIRMED (owner, 2026-09-07/13; ADR-0084, `structure-blocks.md`): no; neither is the Heart's centre 3×3.** The portal discards non-Imps with their gold, including converted goodly creatures, but refuses Imps and enemy prisoners. Imps are discarded through the Heart. Movement blocking is built; Hand disposal wrongly accepts Imps at portals and refuses the Heart, while its prisoner refusal is correct | **Narrowed by the captured cache (2026-09-17, Q352):** for routing the portal centre is byte 4, crossed only with option bit 2 (a leaving creature), and the heart is plain floor throughout; the grid and the Hand keep the blocks |
| Q89 | **Is attraction a boolean?** | **ANSWERED: no, a per-type budget — except the Lair. CONFIRMED both ways, and BUILT (ADR-0147).** The owner saw a 3x5 Temple give no Dark Angel and a 5x5 give exactly **two** while other types kept arriving; the binary contributes **2 iff the Temple holds object 66**, less one per angel held | Contributions: Lair exempt, Temple 2, Prison and Combat Pit their slab count, everything else `TotalCapacity`. **Open:** the `byte[slab+0x0C] == 0xFF` guard on the Prison arm, and the Lair's unexplained 3.0 branch — both recorded as ADR-0147 assumptions |
| Q90 | **How does DK2 weight which creature a portal produces?** `DecreaseEntrancePersentageOfLastSackedCreatureType` and `IncreaseEntrancePersentageOfNewCreatureTypes` are 33 each, naming a per-type percentage that moves | **ANSWERED AND BUILT (binary 2026-09-28, ADR-0272; `portal-creature-action.md`): weighted, and hardcoded.** Both variables are still unread (2026-09-07). The weight is the first attraction room's contribution less one per held creature (Lair: 1.0, the Goblin's lair 3.0). A per-player `CreatureEntranceInfo` row boosts a type noticed after tick 1 (x200, then x9, x8 ...) and can block the last sacked type | Sacking, which sets the block (`0x004DC7E0` via `0x0045A0D0`), is not built |
| Q91 | **What do players 1 and 2 do?** `PlayerIds` (ADR-0043) says 0 is unowned, 1 the hero side, 2 neutral, 3–7 keepers; not what they do | **PARTLY ANSWERED; implementation half FIXED (ADR-0052).** Four shipped portals belong to player 1, eleven hero portals to player 2; neither is dealt a creature pool, so nothing comes out of them here; the original probably drives them by script. `ImpSupplySystem` now gates on `PlayerIds.IsKeeper`. "Harmless because none has a heart" was a claim nothing checked, and not harmless: the free-imp deadline is hashed state and ran for players 0, 1 and 2 on every level — seven scenario hashes moved with no imp involved | Open: what the original does with 1 and 2. `ManaSystem` still runs for every player — "only keepers accrue mana" has no source |
| Q92 | **How does a keeper claim a portal — or any room somebody else holds?** `ConvertRoomHealth` = −4000 and `AttackRoomHealth` = −1500 name the mechanic | **ANSWERED (owner, unprompted; binary and capture), BUILT (ADR-0044, ADR-0336), CONFIRMED:** *"rooms are instantly claimed by imps, not tile by tile."* One tile is worked and the whole room changes hands when its one health falls below 1. The time is the room's: 23 applications for a neutral 3×3 portal at 80%, as the Level 1 capture shows, and 113 for a rival's 3×3 (`room-health.md` §3). `AttackRoomHealth` is the heroes' demolition, not a claim | — |
| Q93 | **Can a dungeon heart be claimed like any other room?** Nothing in the record distinguishes one: heart and portal are both `ownable, room`, differing only in `Attackable`/`HasFlame` | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-09-07) and BUILT (ADR-0162).** A defeated keeper's heart turns to **ruin**: DK2's `0x004E9130` sets bit 0 of the room's `+0x9A` when the room frame finds no heart object, and the same routine collapses hero gates. Here `PlayerState.HeartRuined` is that bit; `TileWorkRules.Applies(world, …)` lets a rival convert a ruined heart and never a live one, and `GoldStore` gives it no capacity. `RuinedHeartTests` and `DungeonHeartTests` pin both | Closed. Whether the ruined room leaves its keeper's room list, which type-25 scripts assume, is Q358 |
| Q94 | **Is a multi-tile bridge claimed whole, like a room?** The owner said *rooms* are claimed whole, and a wooden bridge is a room in every way the data can tell — room terrain, its own `RoomDef`, built and sold like one | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-09-07), CONFIRMED — yes, like a room**, which is what ADR-0044's room-conversion rule already does, so it closes with no change. Taking one tile of a rival's ten-tile bridge hands over all ten. It had been the largest extrapolation in ADR-0044 and was pinned as behaviour in `RoomConversionTests` rather than buried as an assumption. The one structural hint that DK2 might have treated them differently: bridges are `QuadConstruction` where a portal is `ThreeByThree` | — |
| Q95 | What owner does terrain conversion give to unownable ground? | **ANSWERED (binary and Level 1 capture, 2026-09-27): neutral player 2.** See `tile-work.md` and ADR-0045. `TileWorkRules.Convert` matches. `RoomRules.Unmake` is a separate sale path. | Closed for tile work; sale ownership remains unverified. |
| Q96 | **Should DKII.EXE be decompiled, and under what rule?** | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-09-04; ADR-0046): yes, freely, with the output quarantined.** The rule is fact / expression: numbers, formulas, comparisons and control-flow descriptions cross into `docs/research/` in our own words with provenance; decompiler output never leaves `reverse-engineering/`, gitignored but for its README. Binary facts rank below owner observation, and `[Dk2Behavior]` confidence may reach STRONGLY INFERRED on binary evidence but never CONFIRMED. The GOG EULA question is flagged in the ADR, unresolved; nothing binary-derived is distributed. From the strings pass (`executable-strings.md`): not packed, all four Windows timer APIs imported, 72 of its own C++ filenames present | — |
| Q97 | **What actually makes arrivals slow down as a dungeon fills?** Two mechanisms fit the owner's symptom (Q86); only one is implemented | **ANSWERED (binary and stopwatch, 2026-09-07; `portals.md`): the interval itself stretches.** The portal computes `(n × n + EntranceGenerationSpeedSeconds) × variable19` ticks, read off six instructions at `0x004DBBE0`; seven timed gaps from one portal grow 25, 26, 30, 40, 41, 51, 59 s. The rival — a fixed interval with *eligibility* draining — is WITHDRAWN: it rested on a partial read that contradicted itself (literally, it caps level 1 at one goblin, `binary-entrance.md` §4), and the two variables that would weight such a list are dead (Q90). So lair size does not pace arrivals | The remaining divergence is what `n` counts (Q86) |
| Q98 | **Should portal capacity count hero portals, and portals that cannot produce?** The binary sums room types 3 and 25 with no functionality test | **ANSWERED (binary, `binary-entrance.md` §2) and BUILT: yes to both.** `PortalSystem` increments its portal count *before* the `CanProduce` test and counts both room types, so a damaged, centre-less or claimed Hero Portal raises the cap exactly as DK2's does. The quirk is stated on `PortalRules.CreatureCapacity`. The two divergences this row recorded are closed | Nothing — no divergence remains |
| Q99 | **Does zero health mean death, or a window before it?** | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-09-04), CONFIRMED, BUILT (ADR-0051, `creature-death.md`): two timers, three states.** A collapsed creature lies for a window in which an imp can carry it to a bed or prison; then it dies, and the corpse lies for a second window for carrying to a graveyard. Both timers shipped unread: `CreatureDyingStateDurationSeconds` (26) and `DeadBodyDiesAfterSeconds` (23), both 60. Collapse, corpse carry and Graveyard creation are built (ADR-0063/0151). Imps die instantly and vampires reincarnate for a level (Q111/Q112), so `HasDyingState` reads `GenerateDeadBody` | Opened Q107–Q110 |
| Q100 | **Does regeneration depend on what the creature is doing?** Own-land regeneration and lair healing are applied every second regardless of activity, and they add | **ANSWERED for the case that mattered (owner recording, 2026-09-05): no.** A wounded imp was watched regenerating while digging and while running through the dungeon, so regeneration is not restricted to resting, idling or standing still — which is what this project already did, by omission. Still UNKNOWN and narrower than the question was: whether regeneration continues in combat, while held in the Hand of Evil, or under possession (Q152) | Combat balance most of all: 67 a second on an imp is meaningful during a fight and irrelevant outside one |
| Q101 | **What exactly heals a creature in a lair?** | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-09-04), CONFIRMED, BUILT (ADR-0049): asleep, and in its own bed.** A creature arriving through a portal goes to a lair, takes a free tile and puts its bed on it, one bed per tile; injured, it heals while it sleeps in that bed. ADR-0048's proxy was wrong both ways: it healed anything standing awake on any of its keeper's lair tiles. It settled a third thing nobody asked: **a lair's capacity is its tile count** (Q156), not the general rule for rooms (a temple's is its rim, Q60). Built as `LairRules` + `CreatureSleepSystem`. Also confirmed: a skeleton has no lair and Horny is a summon, consistent with `LairObjectId` = 0 on ten creatures | Follow-ups: Q103 (the cycle), Q104 (an unusable bed), Q105 (no bed) |
| Q102 | **When does a creature's hunger clock start?** Implemented: at spawn, counting to the record's `HungerRate` (180 s for a Goblin) | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-04): at spawn.** `CreatureState_Construct` `0x00476B40` (called only from `Creature_Construct`) stamps LastAteTime `+0x48` with the turn rounded down to 8, ORed with the entity id & 7, an 8-turn stagger; HungerNeed starts at 0. The stamp moves when a helping falls due (`0x00477D2D`) and on eating (`0x004639B8`). No pick-up store was found in `.text`. Ours starts at spawn with no stagger. `creature-needs-routine.md` §3 | Sets the pace at which the hatchery matters |
| Q103 | **Is the sleep cycle really 240 seconds awake and 9 asleep?** The Goblin's record says `TimeAwake` 240 and `TimeSleep` 9, both read as seconds | **CORROBORATED by stopwatch (owner, 2026-09-07) but not closed.** From placing its bed to getting into it, four minutes passed — but that included the walk, so the true interval is 240 minus the walk unless the clock starts at the bed. The nap *"seems to last about 9 seconds"*, matching `TimeSleep`. The pairing stops looking odd on the arithmetic: nine seconds at `ModifyHealthOfCreatureInLairPerSecond` 100 is 900 health, well over half a Goblin's 1500. `LairRules.WantsToSleep` / `HasSleptEnough` | Settleable only in the binary: find what resets the last-slept timestamp |
| Q104 | **Why do the Dwarf and the Stone Knight own a bed they can never sleep in?** Both carry a non-zero `LairObjectId` (6, the hero bed) with `TimeAwake` 32767 | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-05), FIXED (ADR-0060): they do not.** The lair attempt is gated on **`TimeSleep ≠ 0` as well as `LairObjectId ≠ 0`**, and both have `TimeSleep` 0, so the original never sends either for a bed and the bed id is dead data. The same gate excludes the Imp, Skeleton, Horny and the seven heroes with no bed id. `LairRules.NeedsBed` tests both fields; `CreatureNeedsOnRealDataTests.TheDwarfAndTheStoneKnightCarryABedIdAndAreNeverSentForOne` holds the pair against the shipped data | — |
| Q105 | **What happens to a creature with no bed?** | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-09-04), CONFIRMED: it becomes unhappy, cannot sleep, then angry, then leaves.** Two consequences. It does not sleep on the floor — it cannot sleep at all, which is what `CreatureSleepSystem` does, so ADR-0049 was right there by luck and is now right on purpose. And the consequence is the anger ladder, a second owner confirmation of it from a second cause (Q9 gave the same sequence for unpaid creatures); `AngerNoLair` is 100 for a Goblin, joint-largest of its four anger sources with `AngerNoFood`. Anger is built — ADR-0050, ADR-0053, `creature-anger.md`, Q106 for the unit | Lair sizing: creatures leave over it, so lair capacity is a real constraint, not a healing detail |
| Q106 | **What is anger measured in?** `UnhappyThreshold` is 50 on all 46 creatures, so it is a percentage — of a ceiling that appears nowhere | **ANSWERED (owner measurement, 2026-09-04), BUILT (ADR-0053, superseding ADR-0050): the ceiling is 32767** (`short.MaxValue`), confirmed by a second method (Q114). A lairless Goblin accruing `AngerNoLair` 100/s went unhappy at ~2:45, angry at ~5:30 — 16 500 and 33 000 against 16 383 and 32 767, inside 1 %. The 2:1 ratio is the stronger half: anger accrues linearly, the angry threshold is 100 % of the ceiling (in none of the 214 variables), and `UnhappyThreshold` really is a percentage. It is the same saturated s16 the record uses for "never" on hunger and sleep (Q12), which is why the six anger fields are signed. `creature-anger.md` | — |
| Q107 | **Can a creature whose bed is sold while it lies dying be rescued?** | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-09-07), CONFIRMED, and already built:** *"if its bed gets sold an imp can't get that creature to bed, if it is mid flight it gets dropped, and then its dying timer runs out and it dies"*. **Refined by the binary (2026-10-09, ADR-0417):** off a Lair, state 45 (`0x00468420`) chooses again, and a creature with no bed goes to the nearest Lair with a free bed (`0x0046E610`) and makes one there (79); the drop is the case with none, as ours now does. | Owner: when you saw the drop, did another Lair have a free bed? |
| Q108 | **Is the flower-petal indicator above a creature the anger meter, or a separate one?** | **ANSWERED: it is health** — CONFIRMED by the owner across two sittings without the question being put directly either time (Q99, Q167): petals are exhausted when a creature drops, and eating replenishes health and *"I think it gets 2 more petals"*. So the flower is a health readout, not the anger meter, which keeps Q106 a stopwatch question. It also buys a unit: two petals is `CreatureCriticalHealthPercentageOfMax` = 20, so a petal is plausibly a tenth of maximum health, and a Goblin's chicken (375 of 1500) is the *"2 more petals"* to within the hedge. The petal count is CONFIRMED at six by Q171 | — |
| Q109 | **Does an enemy creature collapse the same way?** | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-09-07), CONFIRMED — yes:** *"it collapses the same way, first to dying state which gives imps time to drag them to prison if one is available, after that they die and turn to corpse"*. Exactly the pipeline ADR-0051 / ADR-0063 / ADR-0064 built, and it names the **purpose** of the dying window that this project had only inferred: the window is the imps' opportunity, for a friend's own bed or an enemy's prison cell. `MotionlessInPrisonSeconds` (8) and `PrisonModifyCreatureHealthPerSecond` (−3) are parsed and read by the prison rules (Q172, Q173) | — |
| Q110 | **Does anything shorten the dying window?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-07): no — no second hit and no keeper spell can shorten it.** The sole live reader of `CreatureDyingStateDurationSeconds` is the common creature-health routine: the first lethal hit seeds `60 × 4` and raises `CreatureDying`, and thereafter that routine returns without touching the countdown for every ordinary combat, spell and environmental caller. Dying state `0xd9` alone calls it with `−1` in an explicit bypass, to run the clock down; the other bypass callers are another countdown handler, a trigger action setting a spawned creature's health, and loading paths — not attacks. So the 60-second rescue window is inviolable to further damage, confirming the rule already built | — |
| Q111 | **How exactly does a vampire reincarnate?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-16): `FUN_0048FFC0`.** Called from `FUN_004B2CE0`'s death block on the `CanBeResurrected` arm; proceeds only when `level > 1` AND `(creature+0x3EA & 4) == 0` — the bit the **Monk** branch of the attack routine sets around the damage call — then restores the level-scaled maximum health and decrements the level via `FUN_0048AFA0`. Three CONFIRMED owner statements on one routine (`combat.md` §6.3) | Built as ADR-0371 (Q329); the Monk's mark is not set by ours |
| Q112 | **Do the other seven bodyless creatures really vanish on the spot?** | **STRONGLY INFERRED, and it is what we implement.** The owner confirmed it for the **Imp**; OpenKeeper generalises it to every creature without `GenerateDeadBody` by testing that flag before any unconscious handling, which makes the rule cover Skeleton, Horny, Stone Knight, Unique Skeleton, Jack and both Vampires — a summon, three undead, a construct and Jack, all plausible | Nothing today; it would change which creatures can be rescued. Settle by killing a Skeleton and a Stone Knight and watching the floor — if either lies there, the rule is `GenerateDeadBody` plus an exception list |
| Q113 | **Where does a vampire reincarnate?** | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-09-04, retracting their own earlier claim): the sarcophagus at the Graveyard.** The earlier reading as the vampire's own bed came from a pun on `LairObjectId`, not a finding. Two shipped facts back the correction, neither consulted while the wrong claim stood: the Graveyard's `CreatedCreatureId` is 8, the Vampire (only two rooms create a creature; the other is the Prison's Skeleton, 9), and its object ids are 31–34 against the Vampire's `LairObjectId` 26. `Object.cpp` names `Lair` and `TombStone`, and neither *sarcophagus* nor *coffin* is in the executable's strings, so a bed is a `Lair` object whatever art it wears | Deferred until built: it needs a Graveyard tile to land on, not a bed |
| Q114 | **Does the slap count confirm 32767, or is it 65535 at twice the rate?** | **ANSWERED — 32767**, the measurement that closed the mechanic. Timing cannot separate *(100/s into 32767)* from *(200/s into 65535)* — Q47's trap — so the discriminator had to be a discrete delta, which scales with the ceiling where a rate does not. The owner slapped a Salamander (`AngerSlap` 3000): exactly 6 slaps to unhappy, then 5 more to angry. The counts bracket the ceiling — 5-not-6 gives (30000, 36000], 10-not-11 gives (30000, 33000] — and 32767 is the only meaningful number in the intersection; 65535 would need 11 slaps to unhappy. It also fixes the cadence at 100/s | It pins the discrete deltas: slap +3000, hand −250, combat-pit win −16400, gambling −2000/s |
| Q115 | **Is the anger ladder reversible, or does a mood latch?** | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-09-04), BUILT:** *"anger can be cooled down to unhappy, and unhappy can cool down to happy."* A mood is a pure function of the anger value — no latch, no hysteresis — which is what `CreatureAngerRules.MoodOf` already was (`CreatureAngerTests`). The road back is not the −550 cooling, which drains bucket 5: placing the bed wipes the lair bucket at once (ADR-0179). The cooling *condition* is Q123 | **CLOSED (binary, 2026-09-23): anger does not bank below zero** — the adder floors each counter at 0, so the floor is DK2's, not ours |
| Q116 | **Where does a deserter go?** | **ANSWERED and BUILT.** See `rebellion.md` and ADR-0172 for Leave/Rebel portal routing and Rebel's permanent Good conversion when no portal is reachable. The separate temporary spell allegiance words are now modelled, hashed and saved; see `keeper-spell-turncoat.md`. | Closed. The conversion route into a turncoat still needs the torture chamber |
| Q117 | **Water is crossable — but how much does crossing cost, and how often?** | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-09-04), BUILT — nothing, to a creature that can cross it.** The flag that decides passability decides the cost too, so `TerrainDef.Damage` is gated per hazard on `CanWalkOnWater` / `CanWalkOnLava`. `CanWalkOnWater` is on 44 of 46 creatures; the two without are the Vampire and Unique Vampire, named by the owner from memory and singled out by the wiki's water warning. So water is a hindrance with two exceptions, not a barrier with exceptions. Seven creatures cross lava and 39 burn. `Damage` is non-zero on Water (+100), Lava (+100) and **Hero Lair (−67)**: a signed health delta, so the hero lair heals | Magnitude of the slowdown is Q118 |
| Q118 | How much does water slow a creature, and what is the hazard damage cadence? | **Slowdown ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-10):** see steering-behaviours.md §7.5; WaterSpeedRules and shipped-data tests reproduce the modifier. The third-speed hypothesis is disproved. Visible slowdown was observed by the owner; its ratio has not been timed live. | Hazard cadence remains open: time a Vampire in water; 25 s at 100/s versus 1.25 s at 100/tick. Routing is Q30. |
| Q118c | Does level-scaled speed change water's relative slowdown? | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-10):** see steering-behaviours.md §7.5. The modifier uses the current speed after definition loading and keeps the same ratio across levels, apart from integer rounding. Imp levels 1, 2 and 10 are regression-tested. | A live timed comparison remains optional corroboration of the binary reading; the owner's level-2 estimate was 50–60 % slower. |
| Q119 | **What does a level actually do to a creature?** | **ANSWERED from the shipped data, BUILT (`CreatureLevelRules`, `creature-levels.md`).** `GlobalVariables.kwd` carries a 32-stat × 9-level table of percentages against the level-1 value — 320 rows, parsed from the start and read by nothing until the owner mentioned imp speed. Stat ids are OpenKeeper's `StatType`: 1 HEALTH (600 % at L10), 4 MELEE_DAMAGE (600 %), 13 EXPERIENCE_POINTS_FOR_NEXT_LEVEL (1000 %), 21/22 SPEED and RUN_SPEED (250 %). **Ten of the 32 never scale**, own-land regeneration among them. Max health and walking speed are now the levelled values | Quirk kept: stats 10, 11 and 14 read 100 % at every level but L4, where they drop to 25/20/20 %; ADR-0023 wants behaviour before a correction. Gaining a level is Q124 |
| Q124 | **What raises a creature's level?** | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-09-04), BUILT (`CreatureExperienceSystem`):** imps gain experience passively by working (digging, reinforcing, mining); others mostly by training and fighting — level 4 in the training room, 8 in the combat pit, 10 only through actual fights. Two caps are shipped variables to the number — `TrainingRoomMaxExperienceLevel` 4, `CombatPitMaxExperienceLevel` 8 — both previously unread. A worker earns `ImpExperienceGainPerSecond` 20 per original tick while `Working`, 80 a game second, so 37.5 s of digging buys level 2 (Q79); an idle imp and every non-worker earn nothing | The record's `ExpPerSecond` 1200 for an Imp reads as the same rate per minute, which Q208 puts under strain. Training and fighting are unbuilt |
| Q126 | **Is there a cap on an imp's level, and what lifts a creature past 8?** | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-09-07), CONFIRMED: 10, for every creature, imps included** — which is `GameDatabase.MaxCreatureLevel` already, honoured by `CreatureLevelRules.Clamp` and the experience system, so the imp needs no special case. One possible exception the owner named: Horny has no level visible in the game at all and is *"OP"*, which would put it outside the level system rather than at the top of it. Still UNKNOWN: *"actual fights"* is the only route past 8, so combat must award experience directly, and no per-kill or per-hit field has been identified — `ExpPerSecond` is a rate, not a bounty | Combat |
| Q127 | **Is `ExperiencePoints` a lifetime total, or progress towards the next level?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-07), BUILT (ADR-0083) — both earlier readings were wrong.** DK2's adder `FUN_00457880` **zeroes the balance on promotion** rather than subtracting the threshold, discarding the overshoot, and buys **one level per call**, so a large award cannot cascade. Two clauses nobody guessed at: an `ExpForNextLevel` of zero refuses everything, losses included (the field is the switch that makes a creature capable of experience at all), and a loss larger than the balance is a no-op, not a clamp. `CreatureLevelRules.Grant` | The discard makes an award's cadence behaviour: four awards of ten and one of forty leave different numbers on any second crossing a threshold |
| Q128 | **Why does `TypeFood` fall on the Torture Pendulum?** The OBJECTS record's food bit (`0x80`) is set on exactly two of 145 objects: the **Chicken** (9), which is obvious, and the **Torture Pendulum** (65), which is not | **UNKNOWN and harmless.** Either a data-entry accident or a broader meaning for the bit, and two members is too small a set to argue from either way. Nothing depends on it: the rule that matters asks whether a hungry creature can eat a thing, and a pendulum is not on a hatchery tile. Recorded so that a future "anything with `TypeFood` is edible" rule gets a second look | — |
| Q129 | **Is the Dungeon Heart's health 34464 or a truncated 100000?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-05).** 100000. `0x0049B1C0` seeds the object from the u16 Hp (34464); `0x004DB700` then adds var174 − health through `0x004B2CE0` (flat for objects), so the heart holds var174, 100000, and takes off `trunc(health × (100 − pct)/100)` through `0x004DA200`, `pct` being the heart room's percentage; 0 ruins the room and makes no heart. Level 7's rival heart (1) starts at 1000 and Level 8's (0) is a ruin (STRONGLY INFERRED). Built as ADR-0360. Evidence: `win-and-defeat.md` §3 | — |
| Q130 | **Where do object instances live?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-04), BUILT (ADR-0054): one type with a tag, not a family of types.** Its object dump prints a literal `Union Data:` header then one of twenty-three blocks, all reading offset `0x14A`; the next field is at `0x15C`, so the union is exactly 18 bytes of per-kind state. The tag is the object's own definition id, branched on with hardcoded literals, twenty-one of which agree with our parse of `Objects.kwd`. Five type flags are confirmed by the bits the dispatch tests: `TypeGold` `0x40`, `TypeSpecial` `0x04`, `TypeLair` `0x20`, `TypeCrate` `0x10`, `TypeSpellBook` `0x08` — and flags beat ids, which is why one `Lair` branch serves all twelve beds. Ours is a flat `ObjectState` in `WorldState.Objects`; `objects.md` | — |
| Q131 | **Is a bed created when a creature claims a tile, or does it ship with the room?** | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-09-05), CONFIRMED — created on claim.** *"Yes a lair is bare, green lair texture on the floor tile, only when a creature enters a lair and claims a tile by dropping its bed does that tile get furniture on top of it."* The bed object is of the claiming creature's own kind, which the data had implied and nobody had watched: twelve bed objects serve 46 creatures and each names which of the twelve is its, so the object cannot be chosen before the creature is. `LairRules.PlaceBed` / `TryPlaceBedInAnyLair` already did this; the tag moves from STRONGLY INFERRED to CONFIRMED and ADR-0054's one open assumption is discharged | — |
| Q132 | **What is balance variable 19, and what supplies its value?** | **ANSWERED (binary and level files, 2026-09-05), BUILT (ADR-0066): ticks per game second, supplied by the level header.** The loader copies the LEVEL record's 16-bit field two bytes after the trigger id — OpenKeeper's `ticksPerSec` — and all 66 levels carry 4. No VARIABLES record for id 19 exists in any file: its dispatch stub is a second way to set the same global, which nothing shipped uses. Q1 has the three uses that confirm the unit. Trap: an earlier "exactly one writer" finding was wrong — that day's raw scan listed two stores to `0x006F2590` and the write-up counted one | Unblocks every seconds-in-the-table conversion. Nothing re-reads a "blocked on Qnnn" note when Qnnn is answered — see `GOAL_ALIGNMENT.md` |
| Q133 | **What turns a hatchery's egg into a chicken?** | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-09-05), CONFIRMED:** *"it produces eggs that hatch, that entire animation of eggs appearing and hatching lasts about 2 seconds, and after that a chicken appears and roams around the hatchery."* So the step the binary implied is real — the room lays object 47 and it becomes object 9 — and the delay is about two seconds. `HatcheryRules.Hatch` performs the replacement. **The two-second figure is an animation length, not a counter read off anything**; whether the simulation holds an egg that long or the art covers a shorter change is unsettled | Does not matter until something ticks the egg |
| Q135 | **What limits a hatchery's flock?** | **ANSWERED (owner measurement, 2026-09-05), BUILT: one chicken per room tile.** The owner counted exactly 9 in a 3x3, more than 20 in a 5x5. That rules out `MaxFreeRangeChickensPerPlayer` (12), this project's assumption: the variable is real and checked before laying, but it caps *loose* birds, as its name says and as the Chicken union's `FreeRange` field distinguishes (Q65 has the same shape). The record said it all along: the Chicken's OBJECTS entry carries `RoomCapacity` = 1, so `HatcheryRules.FlockCapacity` reads the definition rather than hardcoding nine | Open: whether DK2 refuses to lay when full or lets the surplus rot. Ours refuses, because a 3x3 settling at *exactly* nine is a cap, not the balance point of two rates |
| Q134 | **Why is the hatchery's exponent 0.315477776?** The laying interval is `ChickenGenerationTimePerHatchery / pow(tiles, 0.315477776)`, truncated, times variable 19 | **UNKNOWN why that value; it does not matter for fidelity — the constant is the constant.** It is a double built from two immediates, so it was typed, not computed. It is chosen so nine tiles gives almost exactly 2, but it is not `log 2 / log 9` (0.31546), so that is a reading, not a derivation. The consequence is not zero (ADR-0066): `pow(9, e) = 2.0000566`, a hair above two, so a 3x3 computes `16 / 2.0000566 = 7.99977` and truncation takes **7** where a designer aiming at half would have got 8. It is also why the interval uses a fixed-point table rather than `Math.Pow`, which would diverge a hashed run | — |
| Q125 | **Where does DK2's creature state machine live, and what shape is it?** | **ANSWERED (2026-09-10): a 281-row state table plus 32 category records carrying the enter, update and leave hooks — registered at startup, not statically linked.** Two earlier readings are dead: the "class hierarchy with ~25 leaves" taken from the 25 `*State.cpp` filenames (of 163 surviving RTTI names exactly one, `CState`, is a state) and the vtable route (all eight pointer runs are switch tables). The 17 writes recorded here are the head of a run spanning `0x004798F4`-`0x0047A773`, 512 dwords, reached from a static initialiser at `0x0068e04c` | Shape settled; what is left is the 264 state handlers and `+0x3c` (Q186, Q300). ADR-0135, `creature-state-table.md`, `state-categories.md` |
| Q120 | **Which objects belong to which room?** | **ANSWERED (data): `Rooms.kwd` carries an `ObjectIds` list per room.** Ten rooms have one — Hatchery 35; Library 64, 58; Training Room 14, 29; Guard Room 71, 112; Work Shop 16, 15, 46, 15, 16; Prison 49, 50, 51; Torture Chamber 8, 8, 61, 8, 62, 63; Graveyard 31–34; Casino 87, 59, 67, 68; Combat Pit 49, 50 — and the Graveyard row is what closed Q113. The binary names the kinds: `Object.cpp`'s union dump lists twenty-three, from `Lair` and `TombStone` to the two hero gates (`objects.md`), and a `Lair` object's dump includes a `CreatureId`, so a bed knows whose it is (Q130). OBJECTS is parsed (`KwdObjects`, 894-byte records), so every id has a name | What each object *does* is per-kind engine behaviour and mostly unread |
| Q122 | **Is the angry job drawn once, or re-drawn until it succeeds?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-05), BUILT (ADR-0061): re-drawn.** The chooser rolls `rand(100)` afresh on every allowed selection, tries the job whose range holds the roll, then every job in list order; a new selection waits five game seconds (5 × variable 19). `LastGameTickSuccessfullyChoseAngryJob` is the timestamp it compares against; nothing else is retained. Ranges are inclusive at both ends and the roll is 0..99, so a 50/50 table is really 51/49; both quirks reproduced. `CreatureAngerSystem.Decide`; `creature-anger.md` | One deliberate stop: an unbuilt job ends the selection rather than falling through to `Leave`, so a Goblin rolling `Rebel` waits five seconds and rolls again instead of quietly deserting |
| Q123 | **Does anger cool anywhere in the lair, or only asleep in your own bed?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-07), CONFIRMED — category-gated, awake states included.** Variables 75 (`ModifyHealthOfCreatureInLairPerSecond`) and 76 (the anger twin) are read by one routine at `0x004DFF00`, which heals behind an unread predicate, then applies 76 to anger bucket 5 unconditionally. Its three callers are the per-tick hooks of state **categories** 9, 6, 10, which the 281-state table maps to seven states, **sulking** included. Each fires once a game second and tests nothing about tile or state, so awake states are inside cooling | **Closed 2026-09-23 (ADR-0171):** cooling covers all seven states. Whether awake states gain *health* stays UNKNOWN, so healing keeps the narrow gate |
| Q136 | **Which player does a floor pile belong to?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-25; STRONGLY INFERRED): the neutral player.** All eight calls to the object creator `0x004B7A90` that make a floor pile (type 1) pass `word[0x006F2548]`, which Q266 pinned as the neutral player: dying creatures, sold or destroyed stores, the Hand's untargeted drop, a defeated heart and the computer keeper's gifts alike. Only treasury piles (type 3, two sites) take a room-side owner. Ours already uses `PlayerIds.Neutral`. `gold-on-the-floor.md` §8 | Whether an imp fetches a rival's pile follows from this and the fetch task's owner test; not watched in play |
| Q137 | **Does a creature that leaves through a portal drop its gold, as a dying one does?** | **ANSWERED, CONFIRMED (owner, 2026-09-07/13): no.** Ordinary and converted goodly creatures thrown through a portal leave with their gold. `CreatureAngerSystem.Gone` already removes carried gold instead of spilling it; Hand removal logs `creature_left via=thrown_in`. Imps cannot use this route (ADR-0084) | — |
| Q138 | **Does gold an imp cannot carry stay on the floor?** | **ANSWERED AND BUILT (binary 2026-09-28, ADR-0264; `gold-on-the-floor.md` §10).** Yes. The mining strike `0x004971D0` hands the imp what it can hold and puts the rest down at the imp's position (`0x004510C0`), merging into a pile within 1/64 of a tile. Ours used to destroy it | Closed |
| Q139 | **Which tile does an overflowing refund land on?** | **OURS, not DK2's, and flagged as such at the call site.** The original's give-gold-to-player routine takes a **position** and, once every treasury and heart has refused, makes a floor pile there — so the tile is chosen by the *caller*, and a sell command has a whole rectangle of them. Ours uses the last tile sold | Only bites when a keeper sells several tiles at once with nowhere to bank the refund |
| Q140 | **What does an imp with a full treasury do with its load?** | **ANSWERED AND BUILT (binary and the owner's Level 2 recording, 2026-09-28, ADR-0264; `gold-on-the-floor.md` §10).** A miner with no store to go to keeps mining, and what its hands cannot take goes down at its feet. It gives the seam up once the pile in its third-of-a-tile cell exceeds `MaxGoldPileOutsideTreasury`, and the chooser will not stand it in such a cell. The twelve slots are where an imp stands, not a put-down layout; ours models them (`MiningSlots`). The Level 2 replay still diverges: its imps walk more and mine less than DK2's before turn 635 (`navigation-captures.md`) | Closed |
| Q141 | **Where does "fetch gold off the floor" sit in an imp's job priority?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-05): BELOW marked excavation.** The owner reported an imp taking floor gold to a treasury first, watched with a marked gem tile beside the gold and nobody mining it; that was read as fetching outranking excavation and built so for a day, until the owner doubted their own reading. DK2 keeps a keeper-wide task list whose entries carry a kind, and one function tries the kinds in a fixed order: digging and mining a marked tile are kinds 1 and 2, tried first; a floor object flagged `TypeGold` is kind 7. Q24 stands exactly as ADR-0018 had it. `imp-task-priority.md` | Trap: the "sixth of nine" chain this row used to cite is a per-tile callback, not the priority — Q147 |
| Q142 | **Does an imp with a part-load top up from a pile it walks past?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-05): yes, until its hands are nearly full.** Before the gold kind is searched at all, the original tests the hands: an imp holding at least a level-scaled share of `MaxGoldHeld` (level 1 all of it; higher levels through a percentage table) less a global slack is sent to deposit instead of fetching more; below that it keeps fetching. The deposit routine picks the nearest treasury or heart with room | **BUILT (ADR-0288).** The pre-choice deposit that stood in for this is gone: a part-load stays in the hands (state 27 deposits only when full), and the aimed gold retry deposits a full load. `imp-task-priority.md` §5 |
| Q143 | **Does picking gold up off the floor take time?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-04): no.** Imp state 27 (`0x00466850`) starts no action and waits on none. Not read: whether the walk's arrival adds a turn before 27 runs. `imp-task-priority.md` §5 | Owner: does an imp visibly pause on a pile before turning round? A pause would point at the arrival, not state 27 |
| Q144 | **Why does gold mined on disconnected ground never come home?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-05): unclaimed ground.** The original's gold task refuses a tile that is neither water nor owned by the worker's own player, before looking at what stands on it; the bridge never came into it. `CanFetchFrom` now tests the ground. The prompting observation was withdrawn by the owner (2026-09-06): the gold does come home, because imps wade and mined gold never touches the floor (`imp-tasks.md`). The rule stands — it governs picking loose piles up, not mining — but ours reproduced the outcome by accident, since both its searches have to walk | Why water is exempt is UNKNOWN. Water is never owned, so without the exception gold that fell in would be lost for good |
| Q145 | **Does fetching interrupt a job already in progress, or only the choice of the next one?** | **ANSWERED for the imp's own turn (binary, 2026-10-04): only the next choice.** Dig state 14 (`0x004655D0`) swings again until the wall falls or its task dies, and never looks for gold; the dispatcher runs no needs or mood chooser for a worker. Mining (26) also leaves for full hands, which explains the gem observation: a deposit, not a fetch. STRONGLY INFERRED that nothing outside kicks a digger when gold appears. Ours matches. `imp-tasks.md` | Owner: with gold dropped mid-wall, do imps ever drop a swing on ordinary rock? |
| Q146 | **Where does fetching gold sit relative to claiming and reinforcing?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-05), BUILT (ADR-0058) — it is not a rung.** Gold (kind 7) is in the original's positional tier and is never searched whole-map by kind. Claiming (kind 3) *is* a whole-map kind, so claiming beats gold everywhere; reinforcing (kinds 6/16) shares the positional tier with gold and comes before it on the same tile, so a wall beats gold at equal distance and a nearer pile beats a farther wall. Kinds identified: 3 claim, 6/16 reinforce/fix, 12 corpse→graveyard. `TrySweep` is one nearest-first search asking reinforce-then-gold at each tile | The argument that gold placed last would never be collected failed because the order is not a ladder: gold is not last, it is *near* |
| Q147 | **Is DK2's job priority global, or a tie-break within one position?** | **ANSWERED — BOTH, IN TIERS**, BUILT (ADR-0058). The owner ran the experiment (2026-09-05): *"imps prioritise walls over gold on the floor, in both cases"* — a far wall beats gold underfoot and a near wall beats gold a few tiles off. **Tier A** walks a byte table of kinds (`0e 08 09 01 03 0d 0c` — claim-just-dug, objects, traps/doors, dig/mine, claim, carry creature, corpse) whole-map, one kind at a time; **Tier B**, only when A is empty, ring-scans out from the imp with a four-step per-tile chain (reinforce/fix → gold → objects → kind 11). Our whole-map-per-kind shape was right for digging and claiming and is kept. `imp-task-priority.md` §4 | Distance is steps here and rings in the original (Q25) |
| Q148 | **Does an imp held in the Hand of Evil count towards `MinimumImpThreshold`?** | **ANSWERED — yes, it counts.** CONFIRMED by the owner (2026-09-05): holding one of exactly four imps produces no fifth. A second line agrees — the count the free supply reads is a per-creature-type tally kept on the keeper, moved in exactly two places, when a thing gains an owner and when it loses one; picking a creature up does not change whose it is, so the tally never sees it. Mechanism STRONGLY INFERRED, behaviour CONFIRMED. The imp portrait's figure visibly *excludes* held imps, so DK2 keeps two counts for two purposes and reusing the visible one would have been natural | `ImpSupplyRules.ImpCount` says so at the point where a future Hand of Evil would break it |
| Q149 | **Is the ~1.4 s before a free imp appears simulation or animation?** | **ANSWERED: presentation-shaped, and nothing in the simulation depends on it.** The original casts the Create Imp keeper power to make a free imp, and a keeper power plays a creation effect. The deadline is set at the moment of the **decision**, not when the creature exists (Q56), so the delay cannot accumulate across replacements either way. Ours creates the imp immediately, which is right for the interval and possibly a second early for the appearance | — |
| Q150 | **What actually triggers a creature to go and get a lair?** | **ANSWERED (owner + binary, 2026-09-05), BUILT (ADR-0060) — being idle without one.** The idle state's first act for a bedless creature is *try to get a lair*; the unhappy and angry choosers do the same before rolling, and a sleep need every `TimeAwake` is a second way in. The attempt fails when no reachable lair has a free slot, and only then does the creature take a job, retrying at its next idle decision. Mood was a coincidence: the unhappy chooser tries the lair first too. `lair-acquisition.md` §5, §7, §8 | Prediction the owner can break: with a large empty reachable lair built before the portal fires, *every* creature beds down before its first job |
| Q151 | **What is the extra test that makes one lair room preferred over another?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-05), BUILT (ADR-0060) — alignment.** For each bed in a candidate lair the finder asks whether that bed's owner is of the other side, using the creature-flag pair `IsEvil` / bit 8 — and bit 8 is the exact complement of `IsEvil` across all 46 shipped creatures, so it is now named `IsGood` (replacing `Unk8`). The nearest lair with no such bed wins; the nearest lair of any kind is the fallback. Built as the room preference in `CreatureSleepSystem`; `LairPreferenceOnRealDataTests` holds the complement | Observable only with converted heroes sharing a dungeon with evil creatures, which is late-campaign play |
| Q152 | **Own-land regeneration in combat, the hand and possession** | **PARTLY ANSWERED (2026-10-04).** The creature update has no combat/possession exclusion; see `keeper-spell-possession.md` §Regeneration.
