Original per-turn regeneration cadence remains to integrate. |
| Q153 | **Which terrain field is the reinforce ceiling?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-05): `MaxHealth`.** The dump at file `0x1c2` is zero for all 41 terrains, which killed the offset arithmetic: the in-memory record is not the file record shifted but filled field by field by a loader that reads the 552-byte record at our own parser's offsets into a tagged intermediate and copies each tagged field to its runtime slot, so `+0x1ad` receives file `0x1ff`. Kind 6 (reinforce) wants tile health below half of `MaxHealth`, kind 16 (fix) wants it ≠ `MaxHealth` — for Rock, 165 < 330 fresh — so a wall part-way up is a *fix*, not a *reinforce*, both at the same step of the ring scan. Ours takes the same tiles by type. Offsets: `kwd-terrain-rooms.md` | — |
| Q154 | **Should an imp claim the tile it has just dug first?** | **REVISED 2026-09-29 (binary, ADR-0286): no.** A finished dig or mine raises kind 14 on plain unowned path touching the keeper's floor, but Tier A's search has no arm for 14 (jump table `0x00468770`); the task is taken only by a worker stepping onto it on the way, once no neighbour carries a dig task (Q489). ADR-0058 had it first in Tier A | — |
| Q155 | **Does a nearer imp take a task from a farther one?** | **ANSWERED (binary) and BUILT (ADR-0289).** Each handler, finding a held task, takes it when strictly nearer by its own measure and sets the holder to 10 with its job block kept; nothing protects a task taken this tick (`imp-task-stealing.md` §2, §6). Dig, mine, claim (attack and room conversion included) and reinforce are built; creatures update newest first. DK2 steals 3.0% of takes; ours 6.0% on Level 1 and 3.8% on Level 2, the claim share off both ways (5.1 against 12 a Level 1 game, 31 against 13 on Level 2), which the checker traces to Tier B and gold choices (Q493) | Not built: the drop scan's dig handler (`0x0046A550`, `0x32` slack), gold stealing (object stealing: ADR-0294), the loser walking its old route on |
| Q156 | **How many beds does a lair hold?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-05): one per tile.** The room capacity routine is a switch on room type; for a lair it copies the room's tile counter (incremented as tiles join, summed on merge) into the capacity field, and the free-slot test is `used + 1 ≤ tiles`. The on-arrival predicate blocks a tile holding a bed *of any kind*, so the count and the placement agree. Already what `LairRules.Capacity` does, by inference until now | — |
| Q157 | **Which tile of the chosen lair does a bedless creature walk to?** | **ANSWERED (binary).** The attempt hands the finder's room to the travel-to-room helper (`FUN_00471A70`, `travel-states.md` §2.2), which walks to a point from `0x004E4C10`: a random tile of the room, then the next walkable one (`combat-pit-spectators.md`, `steal-gold.md`; its four cell tests unread). The lair state's entry test only requires *some* tile of the keeper's lair underfoot, and the tile search starts from wherever that is. Built 2026-09-05 as the room tile nearest its bounding-box centre (`LairRules.ArrivalTile`, ADR-0060), so ours differs from DK2's random tile | Where a creature's bed lands relative to the door — the search is local-first, so the arrival tile decides the bed tile most of the time |
| Q158 | **What is creature definition flag `0x4000`?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-05) — it is `IsHorny`, and the enum already said so.** The slap handler tests it first, before every other gate, and sends the creature into a different routine that never reaches the work bonus. Across the 46 shipped creatures the bit is set on Horny alone, and the enum has carried the name `IsHorny` at bit 14 since `creatures_struct.bt` was transcribed — both halves were in the repository and nothing had put them together. Nothing acts on it beyond refusing the slap (`HandRules.CanSlap`, ADR-0062) | Horny is a special case in DK2 in every other way too; this is the flag that says so |
| Q159 | **How long does a slap's work bonus last — 25 seconds or 60?** | **ANSWERED — 25 for the work bonus and 60 for a research bonus: two different effects.** CONFIRMED by the owner off the status bar (2026-09-06). The slap handler writes a hardcoded 100 into `WorkFasterTimer` and decrements it once a tick, which at four ticks a game second is exactly `SlapWorkFasterDurationSeconds` = 25 — a third independent confirmation of the tick rate. The 60 is not dead (ADR-0090): `IncreasedWorkRateDurationFromSlappingSeconds` is read only by the research adder, as the window on a **research** bonus of +200 %. Both are stamped from the same handler's `LastSlapTick` at `creature+0x4BB` | Standing lesson: record a variable as "no reader found", never as "no reader" |

It is **not** the hand's grace period, which the original hardcodes as `PickupTick + 20 ×` variable 19 — twenty game seconds, not a thousand (`hand-of-evil.md` §7) — and that hardcoded twenty is what is documented and built | — |
| Q161 | **Can the hand pick up a collapsed creature?** | **ANSWERED — no, and this project had it backwards.** CONFIRMED by the owner (2026-09-05): neither a corpse nor a creature bleeding out can be picked up; only an imp carries them, to a grave or a bed. | Q162 | **How far does a drop look for work?** | **ANSWERED: three tiles.** The native ring scan receives radius 0x2000 but visits rings 1–3 before its strict bound check. Evidence: `imp-task-priority.md` §4b and `imp-task-objects-and-walks.md` §2 | Matched Secret Door acceptance remains pending |

Take-hold `0x0048B4A0` sets it and the drop `0x0048B500` clears it. It runs on open floor and in a Graveyard. The roll sits after the creature is linked in and occupancy incremented, in a branch taken only for room type `0xb` when safe capacity < occupancy: one `random(100)`, break below `(occupancy − safe capacity) × 50`. The `Rooms.kwd` lists (Q120) are furniture, and no listed object carries one of the three flags, so the shared entries this row asked about (49, 50, 8) are never carried. The unidentified room field ADR-0065 expected is those flags | None |
| Q170 | **Can an imp fetch a loose object off water?** | **ANSWERED: DK2 says yes and this project says no.** The kind 8 handler tests objects owned by nobody, standing on the imp's own floor *or water* — the same pairing kind 7 uses for gold (Q144), already narrowed the same way. An imp here fetches only off its own floor | **Live divergence.** It shows for an object that ends up in water, which nothing in this project can yet arrange |
| Q171 | **How many petals does a healthy creature's flower have?** | **ANSWERED — SIX**, CONFIRMED by the owner with a screenshot (2026-09-06). This project had inferred ten from `CreatureCriticalHealthPercentageOfMax` 20 read as two of ten. The inference was wrong and the number it supported survives: at six petals, 50 % is exactly three and 20 % is 1.2, which reads as two only if the display rounds up — and it must, since a creature at 1 % still shows a petal. No shipped percentage is near 33 %, so "two petals of six" has no better candidate than the 20 already built. The flower also carries level, current activity and mood — its content when `DK2.Presentation` grows one | Q167's loose end is sharper and the game answers it outright: the status bar prints `Health: NN%` |
| Q172 | **What does a prisoner actually do in its cell?** | **ANSWERED AND BUILT (2026-09-06, ADR-0073, ADR-0074).** A prisoner runs a three-state loop in category 14: **114** arrives, **115** decides, **116** walks and pauses. 115 draws `random(4)` as a start index and scans `(start + i) % 4` through the four orthogonal offsets, taking the first candidate in the **same room instance**, which keeps a prisoner in its cell with no separate rule. 116 rolls `random(100) < 20` each tick to go back to 115. Built as `PrisonerWanderSystem`; the chicken race too (ADR-0074, Q195), with nothing reserving the bird | **Left: the keeper's end.** The Hand of Evil holds creature ids and cannot carry a chicken, which the owner confirms it does (Q196), so the mechanic is complete and unreachable in play |
| Q173 | **Is the routine applying the prison drain actually the prison's?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-06): yes.** Creature states carry a category byte, and a 32-record category table holds a mask, enter/update/leave hooks at `+4`/`+0x14`/`+0x24`, a transition mask and the id. Its category-14 update hook is the drain, and 14 is exactly the six prison states `0x72`–`0x77`. It applies `PrisonModifyCreatureHealthPerSecond × 4` every 16 ticks, then `ModifyAngerInCompanyOfHatedCreaturesPerSecond` at reason 9. The general health routine recognises the drain by its amount and suppresses the hurt reaction, so nothing else may deal it | Whether we apply the accompanying anger. Trap: the drain has no callers and read as an orphan; its only reference is a `MOV EBX` immediate at `0x00479f4a` |
| Q174 | **How does a hungry creature reach the hatchery in the original?** | **ANSWERED (2026-09-06): it walks, in state `0x5B`, and there was never a missing piece — the question existed because of an off-by-one.** A finder calls `SetState(0x5B)`; that handler looks the room up again and calls MoveTo with `0x5C` as the resume state; `0x5C` does the eating. It looked broken because the state table was read one record late, filing the walk handler under `0x5A`, which really is near-unreferenced (`creature-state-table.md`). `CreatureEatSystem`'s find-then-walk-then-eat is the original's shape, not a composition of ours. One thing fell out free: a hungry creature in a prison takes `SetState(0x75)` instead, so prisoners feed by a different path (Q172) | — |
| Q175 | **Does a creature wander around the hatchery after eating?** | **ANSWERED, NOT BUILT: the original has a state for it and ours has none.** State `0x5F` picks a random direction with an RNG call carrying `HungerState.cpp` line 367 in its arguments, tests the tile for a blocking flag and walks one tile, retrying up to four directions. Ours goes idle instead. Cosmetic in a simulation with no renderer attached, and not cosmetic the moment two creatures' positions decide who reaches the next bird first | One glance; one method when somebody decides it matters |
| Q176 | **Does a hungry creature wake up to eat?** | **ANSWERED (binary, the same day it was asked): NO.** The needs routine's first line is `if (stateTable[CurrentStateId].groupTag == 7) return 0` — no hunger, no sleep, no pay — and the group tag is the record's field at `+0x16`, not the category at `+0x17`. **Group 7 is not "sleeping":** it is exactly the fourteen states that ARE a need — lair-seeking (79–81), sleeping (82–84), eating (91–95), pay (187–189). So the rule is that a creature already attending to a need does not reconsider its needs, which is broader and gives the same NO. `CreatureEatSystem` leaves a sleeper asleep, pinned by a test | Trap: an earlier write-up called `+0x16` "byte6" and read group 7 as "sleeping"; the two fields were one kind of thing until ADR-0072 |
| Q177 | **Does the aimed bonus carry from one marked tile to the next?** | **STRONGLY INFERRED: yes, and unwatched.** `DroppedTask` has two writers in the whole of `DKII.EXE`, and the clear runs only when a worker still on that kind of task cannot find another anywhere on the map — so a keeper who drops an imp on a marked seam should be paid 12.5 % for the whole seam, not just the first block. Built that way (ADR-0069). The older owner report that a dropped imp *"mines its first seam at 90 % and the next at 80 %"* is consistent with this only if that seam was the last marked work on the map | **The experiment is one glance:** mark two blocks far apart, drop an imp on one, and watch the status bar after the first falls — 90 % and this is right, 80 % and the clear is elsewhere |
| Q178 | **Does an uneaten chicken die and get replaced?** | **ANSWERED from the data (2026-09-07): a chicken dies when no room keeps it — not on a lifespan or a spawn cap.** DK2 has two decay flags — `DieOverTime` (bit 0, anywhere) and `DieOverTimeIfNotInRoom` (bit 1) — and the Chicken (9) carries the second, not the first, as does the Egg (47). A chicken in a hatchery never decays; one with no room around it does, as the owner saw after selling a hatchery. The spawn caps (`MaxFreeRangeChickensPerPlayer` 12, our `FlockCapacity`) stop laying, not living | **UNKNOWN: the decay rate.** No decay-rate variable exists; the Chicken's 1000 health may be what drains. The binary question is specific: what reads `DieOverTimeIfNotInRoom`. The feathers first read as a death are the coop laying |
| Q179 | **The Prison's `RecommendedSize` is 5x5, and a 3x3 prison works** | **CONFIRMED (owner, 2026-09-11): the field is a recommendation, not a minimum, for the Prison.** A 4x4 reads `Captives held: 0 / 5. Status: Open. Maximum Skeletons: 5` — functioning two sizes below its 5x5 record. `PrisonRules.Capacity` (`interior + 1`) is now observed at 3x3, 4x4 and 5x5 | **Answered: the 2x2 graveyard reads 0 / 0**, which its own ring predicts, so no gate is needed to explain any of the three rooms. Q45 is refuted generally |
| Q166 | **How many prisoners does a prison hold?** | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-09-06), CONFIRMED off DK2's own status bar, FIXED the same day (ADR-0070): a 3x3 prison holds TWO and spawns at most TWO skeletons.** This project held one per tile, which would be nine. The owner also established the floor: 3x3 is the minimum usable size; smaller occupies the tiles but does not render or behave as a prison. The arithmetic is readable: the room-capacity routine has a case per room type, and the prison's counts the tiles a "nothing standing here" predicate accepts — everything but the rim — then adds one if non-zero. `PrisonRules.Capacity` is `InteriorTileCount + 1`, so 2 for 3x3 and 0 below | DK2 keeps `TotalCapacity` and `UsedCapacity` as stored room fields and its room dump prints both |
| Q180 | **Do raw addresses survive into the save, and is the entity graph address-linked?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-06): no.** The addresses at `0x20`/`0x28`/`0x34`/`0x3c` are uninitialised stack, not stored pointers: the build stamp is a 64-byte field holding 19 characters of text, and DK2 builds it in a stack buffer without clearing the rest, so every save leaks ~44 bytes of its own stack. The image address is identical in both saves because it is a static; the heap ones differ because different runs wrote them. Entities serialise themselves through virtual calls, so nothing dumps a pointer, and **no fixup pass is needed**. `sav-format.md` | — |
| Q181 | **Which bits of the 10-byte saved runtime tile remain unknown?** | **PARTLY ANSWERED (binary writer `0x0044F3F0`/`0x00452B90`; owner saves).** At true record start: room id is low 12 bits of word 0, health is word 4, owner is low 12 bits of word 6, terrain is byte 8; discovery is bit 6 of byte 9. `Dk2SaveReader` decodes these fields (Q436). | **UNKNOWN:** bytes 2–3, upper bits of room/owner, and remaining bits of byte 9. Identify live tile readers before assigning meaning |
| Q182 | **What is the 189-byte entity record?** | **ANSWERED (2026-09-06), and the question was wrong: there is no 189-byte record and no flat array.** The collection writer at `0x004B6040` emits typed sections — `u32 kindId`, `u32 count`, then that many entities, each serialising itself through virtual slot `+4` — so records are variable-size and per-class. Kind ids 194–197, 200, 202, 203 and 262 are THINGS(190)/SHOTS(260) ids, so the save reuses the data files' own type numbers. The 189 was an artefact: the region is 84.3 % zeros, and even at a proper 11.4 % against a 1.5 % background it is the shadow of mixed sparse sections | Statistics over a container cannot yield a struct. Reading entities needs one serialiser per kind id: eight-plus structs, each read off its routine |
| Q183 | **What is in the fixed block between the header (ends `0x134`) and the grid (variable offset; `mpd1` record start `0x8859`)?** | **PARTLY ANSWERED (2026-09-06): the player's gold is at `0x2dd4`, so the block is per-player state.** Found by searching all five test saves at once for the sequence the owner recorded — 3000, 6000, 6000, 12000, 4500 — which matches at exactly two offsets in the whole file, one here and one inside the entity region. The rest is unread, and the same trick will name more fields from any save series with recorded numbers | Player state. Mana and the creature count are the obvious next two |
| Q187 | **What are the values near the start of the entity region?** | **ANSWERED (2026-09-06): not a header.** Those offsets fall inside entity records. `+0x44` held 8478 and 2597 against header game ticks of 8480 and 2598, so it is a per-entity tick stamp a tick or two behind the save. Folded into Q182 — reaching the rest means the per-kind serialisers | — |
| Q189 | **Why did 44 Rock and 44 Impenetrable Rock tiles appear to have each other's health?** | **ANSWERED (binary writer and owner saves): parser misalignment.** The old reader began eight bytes into a ten-byte record, so its health belonged to the following tile. The writer-aligned reader at Q436 removes the mismatch; it is not a DK2 terrain rule. | Closed; see `sav-format.md` |
| Q184 | **What are the three unread function pointers in each state record?** At `+0x04`, `+0x08`, `+0x0c`, beside the update handler at `+0x00`; most are null | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-07): they are DEAD SLOTS — written by the static initialiser and never read.** A raw scan per slot settles it, since every read of this table carries the base constant. Hits: `+0x00` 3 reads and one store, the handler; `+0x04`, `+0x08`, `+0x0c`, `+0x10` one store each and **zero reads**. The four stores all sit in the table-fill run ending `0x0047BC85`. So four fields are filled that nothing looks at, including `+0x10`, the state id the dispatcher takes from the creature instead | Trap: a guess that they were tail words of an MSVC pointer-to-member predicted these zero reads for the wrong reason |
| Q185 | **What is the flag byte at `+0x18` of a state record?** | **PARTLY ANSWERED: a bitfield, six of eight bits used and five understood.** Bits 3 and 4 suppress the hunger and tiredness clocks, bit 1 the need-anger routine, bit 2 makes the creature not attackable (`0x0045A948`, ADR-0585), bit 6 refuses the fight lookup (ADR-0488). Populations, readers and the Casino run have one home: `creature-state-table.md` §6 | **Unread: bit 0.** Bit 0 is on **195** of 281 with no reader among the seven — the 147 here before counted flag bytes equal to 1, not the bit. Bits 5 and 7 are set by no state, so nothing in this table can recover them |

| Q190 | **Is fog per keeper in the simulation, or per client in the renderer?** | **ANSWERED (2026-09-06, `fog-of-war.md` §11) — ONE bit, ONE viewpoint.** Across a three-save skirmish series, record lane 9 bit 6 is discovery: it clears in a band 13 tiles tall around a 1-tile tunnel, is monotonic across 342 changes, and is set on 100 % of the local player's territory and 0 % of the other three keepers'. No mirror bit exists for any other keeper, and the same scan did recover the owner id's bits, which makes the negative trustworthy. It is not `DistanceCanSee`, `0x4000` for all 46, so the two are different fields and the owner's ~6-tile estimate is confirmed by a number. The footprint is a strict circle: squared fixed-point distance to tile centres, revealed only where strictly less than the range squared. Where they are selected is unread, and whether they are live at all is still open. | Q193 | **Is a tag on an undiscovered tile stored, and where?** | **OUTCOME CONFIRMED AND REPRODUCED (ADR-0076); mechanism UNKNOWN.** The owner (2026-09-06): a fog tile can be marked, it renders as rock until revealed, and the mark is lost on reveal if the tile turns out to be impenetrable rock or a room. `FogOfWarSystem` revalidates a keeper's mark against the revealed terrain's `Taggable` flag at reveal time, which produces both observed outcomes out of the rule `TileTagSystem` already had — encouraging, and still not a reading of what DK2 does internally | The mechanism remains a guess that happens to fit |
| Q194 | Do Sight of Evil and Reveal Map use discovery or a temporary layer? | **PARTLY ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-04).** Sight posts a local presentation region whose radial predicate temporarily bypasses render fog; it does not permanently explore tiles. See `keeper-spell-sight.md`. Reveal Map (special 118) sets the explored bit on every tile for good, for the local player only (`0x00451EF0`, `specials.md` §4) | Sight's presentation clock is Q534 |
| Q534 | What advances the presentation bridge clock used by Sight, and how does it map to simulation ticks/seconds? | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-05): the game turn.** The presentation is the bridge's sink (`0x00527040` stores the container at sink+4), so Sight reads the word `0x00517490` returns. 64 units are 64 turns, visible while turn ≤ start+64; 16 game seconds at the loop's four turns a second (STRONGLY INFERRED, `trigger-state.md` §6). `keeper-spell-sight.md` §2 | - |
| Q195 | **A chicken dropped into a prison: who gets it?** | **ANSWERED AND BUILT (ADR-0074).** DK2 gives it three states, all still *being held captive*: **115** decides (hungry, and past `TimeBetweenReattemptingHungerSeconds`), **117** walks the prison's own tile chain for objects carrying flag `0x80`, ranks by straight-line squared distance and issues a move to the nearest — a hunt, not a meal on the spot — and **118** eats it, paying off the whole of `HungerFill`. The race is the absence of a reservation: nothing marks the chosen bird, so the loser arrives at an empty tile and 118's loop drops it back into 117. An empty cell charges a quarter of the tabled `AngerNoFood`, because the loader stores every `angerNo*` field per DK2 tick. `prison.md` §7 | — |

Gold lifts only from owned claimed land, a cursor rule: the action itself tests no ownership (`0x005135B0`, `0x004C0CB0`; ADR-0310). `CanBeDroppedOnAnyLand` is dead | A chicken or gold dropped onto a creature feeds it or clears its whole wage debt, then is gone: BUILT (ADR-0217, `hand-drop-earmark.md`), delivered in the drop's tick where DK2 waits for the landing. | Q209 | **Does an object become an impassable tile in DK2?** | **ANSWERED (binary, STRONGLY INFERRED): no.** The nav-byte setters are `0x004D1930`/`0x004D1970`; their complete caller graph supplies terrain categories, door owner/state overlays, and three room-side values. No path reads an object definition or sets the blocked bit for furniture. Furniture instead remains continuous collision geometry: `0x004D12B0` gates objects on `Obstacle` and its consumers use position/radius. This accounts for the confirmed routing around beds, bookcases and anvils without a tile-cache write. See `structure-blocks.md` §5.4 |

| Q215 | **What guards DK2's "health reached MaxHealth" conversion?** | **ANSWERED: nothing in the applier.** After the zero-health branch, `0x004532D9`–`0x004532E9` compares the new health with `MaxHealth` and loads `MaxHealthTypeTerrainId` without testing work or delta. The caller invariant is sufficient: `RepairTileHealth` and fill-in have no path, while wall/room repair targets a terrain whose max-health type is itself. `CompletesUpward` models those reachable callers; it is not a binary guard. STRONGLY INFERRED. `tile-work.md` | — |
| Q216 | **Does slapping a researcher visibly triple their research?** | **UNKNOWN, and it needs the research tree to be watchable.** The adder computes the slap bonus as `variable19 × 50 × variable19 × perTick / 100`, which is eight times the per-tick amount added once a game second on top of a base of four — 12 a second against 4, where the variable is named 50 %. The extra `variable19` is read from the instructions (`IMUL ECX,EAX ; IMUL ECX,EBX`, with the divide by 100 as the compiler's `0x51EB851F` reciprocal), not from a decompile, so the arithmetic is not in doubt; what is unverified is that a player experiences it. Built as read (ADR-0090) | Nothing today — the accrual has no consumer. It matters the moment the tree exists, because a 3× slap bonus changes how a level is paced |
| Q217 | **What sets the keeper's casino flag at `player+0x20D`?** It is not a flag: DK2's player dumper calls it `CasinoBigWinnerGameTickRemaining` | **ANSWERED (2026-09-11): nothing does, and the +140 % bonus is unreachable.** Eight instructions in the image mention `0x20d`; four address a player at word width — one *writes zero* (an initialiser that also zeroes `+0x20F`/`+0x213`), one is the dumper, two are gates that skip their bonus when it is zero. Nothing sets it non-zero and nothing decrements it | **Residual:** a write through a folded base would not match a literal-displacement scan and was not searched. `library-and-research.md` §3 |
| Q218 | **What passes DK2's research adder a negative amount?** `FUN_004c2dc0` has a full clamp-subtract branch for one | **ANSWERED (2026-09-11): nothing can — the branch is dead.** A scan of all thirteen initialised blocks, for a rel32 call and for the bare address in a handler table, finds one caller (`0047b084`) and zero absolute occurrences. That caller floors its amount at 0, so research cannot be lost | Nothing. `library-and-research.md` §3 |
| Q219 | **Is there any way in DK2 to turn a floor tile back into rock?**  | **ANSWERED: NO — there is no fill-in mechanism at all** (2026-09-07; the owner was right). (1) `FillInHealth` (`0x006F2568`) has **no reader**: the setter store is its only occurrence in all thirteen initialised blocks. (2) "Fill in" is not DK2's word: one bare `fill` in the executable and zero hits across 507 data files — the name is OpenKeeper's label, inherited unchecked. (3) No fill-in task exists: the candidate handler reads `RepairWallHealth` behind a repair predicate | `TileWorkKind.FillIn` is removed, and `RepairTile` with it, since `RepairTileHealth` has no reader either. `RepairWall` is an imp job since ADR-0387. Supersedes Q22; withdraws Q214; see answered Q215 |
| Q220 | **Does a standalone GLOBALS(0) chunk exist, and if so what is its payload?** | **UNKNOWN outside the shipped v1.70 corpus.** An exhaustive pass over 408 editor KWD/KLD files finds no type-0 chunk and no type-0 LEVEL path row. The known representation is a LEVEL path discriminator, which parses and round-trips synthetically | Nothing in the shipped campaign; mod / custom-map compatibility only. Method: obtain a custom map that enables global overrides, then inspect its LEVEL path and referenced file before defining any standalone payload |
| Q221 | **Is the 16-byte DeadBody THINGS payload correct in retail data?** | **HYPOTHESIS.** Tag 202 is absent from all 6,318 shipped THINGS records. The 16-byte layout is arithmetic over OpenKeeper's field list and has no independent shipped sample | A custom map that starts with a corpse; no shipped level. Method: export a custom editor map with a placed dead body, then run `dk2tools kwd tagged` and `kwd records` against it |
| Q222 | **Where do a level's 1152 action points live, and does anything change them at run time?** | **ANSWERED, then half withdrawn.** ADR-0103 followed the stores: **none of actions 16, 17, 27 and 31 writes an action point.** The shared `Available` byte was four parameter views transcribed alike. A point is still hashed state, for its passage counter and wait timer, but not for the reason first given. The record is read (id, start/end rect, next-waypoint id, wait delay, flags, trigger id, name); level 1 has 27 | Closed by ADR-0103, which also opened Q235–Q238 for the flag meanings and the counters |
| Q223 | **When a ROOM record and the MAP grid disagree about who owns a structure, which does DK2 believe?** | **ANSWERED — CONFIRMED (owner, 2026-09-16): the grid.** On *Cherish* the two gates at map (11,8) and (20,8) read **unclaimed, and not destroyed**. The sweep predicted it: all **133** hero structures carry record player **1** without exception while the grid takes 1, 2, 4 and 5, so a constant cannot be the owner (`placed-things.md` §4) | Closed. Room placement, when it is built, takes the owner from the grid. "Not destroyed" also rules out grid owner 2 meaning a ruined structure |
| Q224 | **What state does a door or a trap need?** | **ANSWERED for storage (ADR-0106, `doors-and-traps.md`):** `WorldState` holds all 940 doors and 959 traps in THINGS-file order, with every parsed field copied verbatim, hashed and snapshotted. They occur across 48 levels; all 940 door `TriggerId` values are zero. Only behaviour and possible runtime field transforms remain UNKNOWN (Q249–Q253) | Closed. The corpus pin compares every projected field with its source and leaves only hero parties and one effect generator unprojected |
| Q225 | **Do DK2-authored files corroborate the trigger types absent from the shipped corpus?** | **UNKNOWN from data.** DKII.EXE has body-read paths for generic types 21, 26, 35, 37, 45, 49, 67 and 74 and action types 36, 39, 40, 41, 46 and 48; event/no-body paths also mention generic 33, 43, 59 and 61 and action 3, 5, 25 and 29. None occurs in the 9,161 shipped records | Custom editor maps; not M6 for any shipped level. Method: create one trigger of each type in the original editor, then use `dk2tools kwd trigger-types` and `kwd records` to confirm the serialized type and body before relying on a handler's dormant layout |
| Q226 | **What do the remaining transcribed trigger selectors mean?** | **PARTLY ANSWERED.** Action 6 bytes 1/2 select availability kind and type (room, creature, door, trap, spell), read through all five callees and built in ADR-0161. Still **UNKNOWN names, known accesses**: generic `TargetId`/`Flag` in 1, 2, 27, 28, `TargetId` in 29, both selectors in 53; action `MakeType`/`TargetId` in 9, `Flag` in 7, `Value` in 30/50, `TargetId` in 42/47 | Continue from each arm's first callee and identify its destination independently; shipped bytes remain preserved meanwhile |
| Q227 | **What are the trigger fields DKII.EXE reads without revealing a role?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-04).** Condition 23 b02: 0 total killed, n player n's slot (0x004C7DF0). Action 24 reads only its 2nd dword's low byte (0x004C8688): 0 speaks and posts a message record, non-zero only speaks, unless Message_Post suppresses or dedups. 35 b00: 0 vanish (no corpse, no gold), 1 death fall, else no-op. 39 b00 door type, 40 b00 trap type, 41 w00 effect id (owner the local player). `trigger-action-arms.md` | Owner: does a repeated action 24 replay its speech? The reader names the fields (ADR-0383) |
| Q228 | **When is a trigger tree evaluated, and what unit is the timer clock?** | **ANSWERED.** Trees run once per game turn, and timers count ticks. The container clock, its constructor default and live cadence measurement are recorded in [trigger-state.md section6](trigger-state.md#6-where-evaluation-happens--once-per-game-turn); the default-rate attribution was corrected during ADR-0596. | **Closed 2026-09-09:** the24slots are the level party table; see `trigger-tree.md`. |
| Q229 | **What is flag id 128?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-04): LevelScore** (world dumper; `+0x72A6` LevelScoreSet). Action 7 id 128 sets, adds or subtracts it, no floor (ours: `TriggerState.Write`); condition 1 id 128 compares it with Value directly (0x00518270); slot +0x344 (0x00511650) adds MpdScore, floored at 0; the HUD 0x00427BA0 shows a widget once set. `trigger-state.md` §4. The condition is built as ADR-0374; an indirect 128 is declined | No +0x344 adder |
| Q230 | **What does DK2 do when a trigger names a flag id above 128?** The write path masks the id with `0xFF` and bounds it no further, so ids 129..255 would index past the 128-byte flag bank into the timer bank that begins `0x80` bytes later | **ANSWERED for the campaign: no shipped level does it** — asserted over all 66 campaign levels in `TriggerStateOnRealDataTests`. `TriggerState` therefore throws rather than reproducing the overrun | Only reachable with a custom map. Author one trigger writing flag 200 and watch whether a timer visibly resets. **Faithfulness and sanity conflict here**, so this is a decision to take deliberately if a real map is found doing it, not a gap to close speculatively |
| Q231 | **Are `0x006E5050` and `0x006E5054` the same object?** Both hold a pointer passed to a constructor, and both are used as `this` for the same world action dispatcher `0x00518AD0` | **ANSWERED by direct observation, 2026-09-08: yes.** Read out of the running process, both point to `0x006F2A08` in both captures: two caches of one pointer to the static world object, not two subobject pointers and not multiple inheritance. So a `this` from either is the same object, the flag and timer banks live on it, and ADR-0102's trigger-state scope conclusion is sound. Statically, `0x006E5054` had already been identified by Q1 as the game object with exactly two writers, `FUN_004C1E20` and `FUN_004C2010` | **Two sessions of static analysis could not settle this; one read of live memory did** |
| Q232 | **What is timer id 16?** Not a timer: the condition path compares it against `0x10` at `0x005184EB` and on a match reads through virtual slot `+0x34C` | **ANSWERED and BUILT (binary 2026-09-26, ADR-0235): the level time limit.** Slot `+0x34C` (`0x00509660`) returns `world+0x72AA`, which DK2's world dumper names `LevelTimeLimit`. Action type 23 sets it; the per-turn world update counts it down and sets world-option bit `0x08` at zero, and in game modes 2 and 3 every keeper then loses (`level-time-limit.md`) | Closed; the countdown's `+0x284` gate is the window-focus suspension (`level-time-limit.md` §2) |
| Q233 | **Is a trigger timer threshold converted from seconds to ticks at level load?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-08), CONFIRMED LIVE: YES.** At `0x0050C2DB` in the level trigger initialiser `FUN_0050B5B0` (caller of the second walker `FUN_00519A20`; its own callers `FUN_0050E620`, `FUN_0050B3C0`, `FUN_00513870`): it walks the condition array at a 20-byte stride, reads the type at `+0xE`, and for types 2 and 34 multiplies the dword at `+4` by variable 19 in place; a second does actions 16 and 23. So the `.kld` holds seconds, the loader scales once, the comparison takes ticks. Live, level 1's 22 type-2 thresholds all read the authored value × 4 | **Our loader must convert exactly once**, and since the original rewrites the record in place, the scaled value is what a save carries |
| Q234 | **Is the root trigger id at `+0x7310` level-constant, and can it be zero while a level runs?** Two captures of level 1 read 89 and then 0, at the same point | **ANSWERED 2026-09-08: level-constant — there was no anomaly, the tool was attached too early.** A fourth capture caught it: the tick counter reset from 77 to 1 three and a half seconds in, and `+0x7310` went 0 → 89 with `+0x731A` following 0 → 1, the initialiser writing both. Before that the tool read the *front end's* arrays, because `FrontEnd3DLevel` is itself a level — a non-null pointer was not proof of a loaded level. So 0 means no level is initialised yet | Nothing in code — `TriggerState` models the banks, not the tree. **Lesson: an ordering precondition handed to the operator is a bug in the tool** |

## The Hero Portal — Q86's last amendment, and four questions numbered after Q234

**Q86 closed further, not reopened** (owner, 2026-09-07): *"hero portal IS AN ENTRANCE,
but for goodly heroes (rival faction)"*, and *"a keeper claims a hero portal room… it just
becomes an indestructable dead room that can't be sold or repurposed."* Both CONFIRMED, and
both were partly divergent here. See `portals.md`, "The Hero Portal is an entrance too".

Portal (3) and Hero Portal (25) have **byte-identical** room flags, terrain flags and
41-byte handler records, so DK2 runs **one** implementation for both. What separates them
is a single condition in the candidate builder — `def+0x686 & 0x400` when the room is
type 3, `def+0x68a & 8` when it is type `0x19` — so the two rosters are **exclusive by room
type, not by owner**. The dead-room properties need no new rule: no `Buildable` flag means
`CanSell` refuses it and nothing can be built over it (Q207), and the absence of the
room-level `Attackable` flag is the "indestructible".

Two divergences found and fixed. (a) `CanComeFromPortal` took no room, so a keeper's
claimed Hero Portal produced the keeper's *evil* creatures — the doc comment on that method
had predicted the bug and dismissed it as costing nothing. (b) Capacity counted only
portals that could produce; DK2's counter tests the room type and nothing else, so a broken
portal and a dead claimed Hero Portal both still raise the cap (Q98).

**Player-facing name:** the game calls an unclaimed one a **"Mercenary Portal"** (Q211).
There is no "Hero Portal" string in the game at all — that is the editor's label in
`Rooms.kwd` 25, which is where `RoomTypeId.HeroPortal` and
`CreatureFlags2.AvailableViaHeroPortal` take their names. Identifiers stay; presentation
text should say Mercenary Portal.

| # | Question | Status | Blocking |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q210 | **What are the other three bits of CREATURES `0x1545`, and which slots do the room-table callers use?** | **(a) LARGELY ANSWERED; bit 2 was a live divergence (ADR-0087).** Bit 3 is `AvailableViaHeroPortal` (Q211). **Bit 2 is "never becomes angry"**, carried by Skeleton and Jack, read by the wrapper every anger site calls. Ours had inferred `CanBeAngered` from the Skeleton's all-zero `angerNo*` fields — true, and wrong for Jack, who could be **calmed by slapping**. **Bit 0** (King Reginald, Stone Knight, Jack) is **Lightning immunity** (`0x004af1e0` is shot process type 9, `shots.md` §8); **bit 1** (Stone Knight) has no reader in either block. **(b) CLOSED:** all twelve indexing sites of the room handler table have their slot; slot `+0x25` is a `JobType` (`portals.md`) | — |
| Q211 | **Does a keeper on Frosty's Castle really get Knights from a claimed Hero Portal?** Its only entrances are four unowned Hero Portals, and it pools exactly the nine `AvailableViaHeroPortal` creatures for keepers 3–6 | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-09-07), CONFIRMED; prediction held:** mercenary units arrive as the keeper's own units, no Dwarf among them. Creatures come out, so the gate is the **room type**, not the owner, and ADR-0086 amendment 2 is right. No Dwarf, so the flag is bit 3 of the last dword, not bit 30 of the first, which selects the same nine *plus* the Dwarf. Four lines agree: the binary's gate, the roster match across all 5449 record bytes, the level's pool, and observation | `PortalOnRealDataTests` pins it; before the fix our simulation produced nothing on that map |
| Q212 | **What does the sole reader of variable 112 do?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-13), STRONGLY INFERRED: it is converted-creature prayer mana, not sacrifice.** The Temple prayer hook applies healing, derives mana from the creature definition and level, and for instance flag `+0x3EA` bit 4 applies `x += x × (112 + 100) ÷ 100` before crediting the player. The manual independently says converted enemies generate twice the mana while praying, so shipped zero intentionally doubles it. No sacrifice routine reads 112; its inherited name is wrong. `sacrifice.md` | Rename `PercentageOfManaGainedBySacrificing` when Temple prayer is built |
| Q213 | **Does anything read `ObjectDef.ManaValue`?** It is zero in all 145 object records | **ANSWERED (2026-09-07): CUT.** The objects loader `FUN_0049DD60` has source delta **file + 0x80**, from aligning thirteen known object file offsets against every loader's source offsets — 13 of 13 on one delta. `ManaValue` (file `0x34E`) lands at runtime `+0x108`, and every word-width access there is a store, with zero word-sized reads. With all 145 records zero, that is CUT, not disabled. Caveat: dword reads of `+0x108` exist, covering `ManaValue` low and `TooltipStringId` high | Trap: the creature loader uses file + `0x3F0` and this one does not; a query for the predicted `0x73E` returned zero hits with a positive control passing, so the mismatch was real. `record-field-sweep.md` |

## Action-point questions raised by ADR-0103

| # | Question | Status | Blocking |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q235 | What do action-point flag bits 0, 2, 3, 4 and 5 mean? | **PARTLY ANSWERED.** The loader copies exactly these five bits (`0x3D`). Direct readers show bit 0 lets one creature routine use the top-left tile, bit 4 drives an inclusive rectangle update, and bit 5 lets one world lookup return the matching point id. No direct gameplay reader was found for bits 2 or 3. Source bit 6 occurs in shipped data but is discarded | Trace the three known readers to observable operations, then put a data watchpoint on runtime `+0x32` for computed readers. Author maps varying one bit. Do not import names from another reimplementation |
| Q236 | Who consumes or mutates the passage counter and wait timer? | **ANSWERED NEGATIVELY.** Runtime `+0x2E` starts at zero; `+0x30` receives authored `WaitDelay × variable 19`. Both are named by the binary's diagnostic path and preserved by its serializer, but a complete direct-offset scan found no gameplay access after load | Put hardware watchpoints on both words using points with distinct delays. Trace the action-point tick virtual path as the static fallback; computed pointer access is the explicit hole in the negative scan |
| Q237 | When is an action-point rectangle tested, and what is its actual per-tick cost? | **Cadence and per-tick cost ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-09): the tick does no rectangle work.** It is vtable slot `0x14`, driven once a game turn from the world frame (Q239), and walks the point's trigger root and nothing else; the loop's second pass hits a `return 0` stub. So **rectangle cost is not per-turn**. `action-points.md`, "Rectangles" | **One invoker named (2026-09-11, ADR-0144): action type 17's arm walks a point's rectangle once when it runs.** Still open: the others. Compare controlled 1×1 and large rectangles with creatures crossing; a cost that scales with area means a spatial notification path |
| Q238 | How is `NextWaypointId` traversed, terminated, and handled when missing or cyclic? | **STRONGLY INFERRED.** It is loaded, diagnosed and serialized; no post-load writer was found, and action 31 does not relink it. 475 of 1,152 shipped points have a non-zero value, so a static directed link is strongly inferred | Trace a creature command targeting a linked point while watching runtime `+0x33`, then author missing-target, self-loop and two-node-cycle maps. Keep mutation out of the model unless a writer is observed |

## Partial knowledge on revealed ground — a sixth rule, and it is not fog

`Text.str` 2471 reads *"This room is owned by another power. Its details are unknown."* — a
non-owner sees no capacity or occupants where the owned-room tooltip prints both. That is a
**second partial-knowledge rule operating on already-revealed ground**, and it means the
presentation layer needs a viewing player whether or not fog is ever built.

## A method correction that outranks any single row

The zero-hit `MaskScan` run that produced *"reached only through a vtable"* (Q86) was not a
coverage limit: the script prepends `0x` to its argument and was handed `0x006a1e9f`, so it
searched for `0x0x006a1e9f` and **reported zero hits rather than an error**. Re-run
correctly it returns 114 hits. See `RESEARCH-QUEUE.md`. A second instance of the same shape
landed the same day: reasoning from record layout put the hero flag at file `0xF00`, which
holds a 3000/2800 value. Both were fixed by **searching for the roster the mechanism must
select** rather than for the place it ought to live — `dk2tools kwd creaturebits` does that,
and is the reusable half.

## The other three trigger walkers — Q239–Q242, Q246–Q248

The world walker is one of four. Campaign level 1 keeps almost none of its script
in it, which is how these got numbered: implementing condition 34 and action
type 14's arming half moved nothing measurable, because trigger 89's chain is two
party spawns and the level runs off its **player** root, trigger 1. Q239 is
answered and its detail is in `trigger-walkers.md`; the row stays for what it
left open.

| Q | Question | What is known | How to settle it |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q239 | **What drives the player, creature and action-point trigger walkers, and how often?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-09): all four run once per game turn, from the same world frame** that Q228 closed — world tree at `0x0050A75B`, party roots at `0x0050A778`, every entity through vtable slot `0x14` at `0x0050A7D7`, every player at `0x0050A7F5`. Root ids and repeat arguments in `trigger-walkers.md`. Each entity walker has a second caller, an **event filter** keyed on a record's type byte, which is not a tree walk and is not driven by the frame | **Still open: the events themselves.** 26 call sites fire the two filters, one behaviour each, and none can be built before the behaviour that fires it. Nothing blocks the per-turn path, which is what level 1 uses |
| Q240 | **What is bit `0x200000` of `+0x3E6`, which lets action type 14 arm nothing and still report success?** | **ANSWERED.** With body byte `01` (`PartyType`) non-zero, the handler scans an entity list for a match on the party id and returns 1 early when that bit is clear (`0x00519FE4` to `0x0051A747`). 244 of 259 shipped type-14 records carry `PartyType` 0 and cannot reach it; 14 carry 2 and one carries 1 | Decompile the entity type whose `+0x417` the scan matches, then watch the bit across a level that ships a non-zero `PartyType`. Our executor models the byte-zero path only and says so |
| Q241 | **What sets bit `0x40` of the world byte at `+0x7239`, which skips the level initialiser's party-disable sweep entirely?** (`0x0050C280`) | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-30): a restored save.** The KWD load `0x0050E620` clears `0x40` before calling `0x0050B5B0`, so a fresh level runs the sweep; the file restore `0x0050E920` holds `0xC0` across it, so a restored level keeps its saved party state (`trigger-tree.md`). The threshold scaling at `0x0050C2DB` is not behind the bit; whether a save carries scaled thresholds is unread | A new level runs the sweep, matching DK2. A restore skips it, the creature roots' repeat snapshot and creature event 3 (`0x0050BCC4`, also behind the bit) (ADR-0550, built) |
| Q242 | **What are the four interface fields player-context condition type 72 tests?** Its arm `0x004C84B8` returns 1 unless `+0xDD8`, `+0xDFA` or `+0xE20` is non-zero or `+0xDA8 == 0x12` | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-30): camera fields, not modals.** `Camera_Update` (`0x0044BED0`): `+0xDA8` is the camera mode (0x12 a scripted path); `+0xDD8`, `+0xDFA`, `+0xE20` are deadlines of timed camera moves, zeroed on arrival. Type 72 is "the camera is still". **We take it as true** (`TriggerRunResult.Assumed`) | "Safe while nobody plays" was about modals: a trigger that moves the camera (a level intro) makes type 72 false unplayed. Find which trigger actions start each move |
### The area walker — Q246–Q248

The action-point walker and five of its seven condition types ship. It walks 15
roots on campaign level 1 and moves nothing there, which is the finding rather
than a defect: both flags its trees write need a human (`docs/EXPERIMENTS.md`).

| Q | Question | What is known | How to settle it |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q246 | **In what order does the world frame reach the action points?** `FUN_004B7830` walks eight `u16` list heads at world `+0x08` through the thing table at `0x006EE540`, and creatures are in the same lists | **ANSWERED, corrected by Q330 (binary, 2026-10-04): not interleaved.** `0x004B7830` walks the lists in index order, each newest first; creatures are list 0 and action points list 5, so every creature tree runs before any action point and the points run in reverse THINGS order (`trigger-walkers.md`). Built with Q330 (ADR-0361) | — |
| Q247 | **Is the tile's 12-bit owner field the player id?** `FUN_004B07E0` masks tile `+0x08` with `0xFFF` and compares it against the `u16` at player `+0x04`, not against a player id | **STRONGLY INFERRED.** Our tiles hold a player id and the two conditions that read ownership compare against it. The shipped values agree wherever we have checked, but the indirection through the player object means they need not | Read player `+0x04` for each of a level's players out of the running process. If it is not the id, the mapping is a lookup and every ownership condition needs it |
| Q248 | **What does an action point whose rectangle is inverted or overhangs the map do?** `FUN_004B07E0` and `FUN_004B0A00` compute an unsigned width and stride the tile array with no bound check | **ANSWERED for the campaign.** An inverted rectangle would give a huge count and read past the array. No shipped point has one, so this is unreachable in the campaign. We skip off-map tiles, which differs only for such a point | A custom map with an inverted rectangle and a type 40 condition on it. Until then, ours is a guard rather than a claim about DK2 |

## Door and trap questions raised by ADR-0106

| Q | Question | What is known | How to settle it |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q249 | **What do doors and traps do at runtime?** | **BUILT, STRONGLY INFERRED (binary, 2026-10-03).** Construction, opening and route costs (ADR-0141/0159/0250, `doors-and-traps.md`); door contact and the door fight (ADR-0318, `door-blocking.md`); a collapsed gate is ruined (ADR-0320); every shipped trap fires (ADR-0321/0331/0333/0337) with upkeep (ADR-0326) and the Troll/Thief/Rogue/Guard reveal (ADR-0338); a creature with nobody to fight attacks a revealed enemy trap (ADR-0340, `CombatTargetRules.ScanTraps`). Disarming is deferred: no fresh-game initiator found (ADR-0329, Q455). `traps.md` §2–§12 | Owner observations Q451, Q452, Q513, Q520, Q524; shot heights with M9 (Q398) |
| Q250 | **Which `DoorThing.Flags` bits survive loading, and what do they mean?** | **ANSWERED AND BUILT (`doors-and-traps.md`, ADR-0141), STRONGLY INFERRED.** Bit 1 seeds `UnderConstruction`; bit 0 requests runtime lock mode 3. The authored byte remains verbatim, but no higher bit is semantically read by the loader. Three of 940 shipped doors set bit 1 | Closed for loading. Door lock behavior remains with Q249 |
| Q251 | **Does DK2 transform `TrapThing.NumberOfShots`, and what consumes it?** | **ANSWERED AND BUILT (`doors-and-traps.md`, ADR-0141), STRONGLY INFERRED.** Zero leaves the constructor's construction bit set; nonzero clears it. No value is copied as a shot count and no firing path consumes it. All 959 shipped authored traps are nonzero and start completed | Rename the parser field only as a separate data-API decision; firing remains Q249 |
| Q252 | **What are `DoorThing.Unknown_0008` and `DoorThing.Unknown_0011`?** | **ANSWERED IN PART (`doors-and-traps.md`), STRONGLY INFERRED.** The dword and each of the three trailing bytes affect the raw-record checksum but are not copied into the runtime door or its saved range. Their semantic meanings remain UNKNOWN; authored state retains them verbatim | Search non-loader consumers of the raw THINGS storage only if a behavior needs their meanings. Do not infer names from their absence in runtime |
| Q253 | **What are `TrapThing.Unknown_0008` and `TrapThing.Unknown_000F`?** | **ANSWERED IN PART (`doors-and-traps.md`), STRONGLY INFERRED.** Both affect the raw-record checksum but neither is copied into the runtime trap or its saved range. | Q260 | **What is the player dword at `+0x15A`, read by condition type 31?** | **ANSWERED AND BUILT: `GoldMined`** (ADR-0112). Which keeper `[0x006F254C]` names is Q266 |

So the player frame's gold sum skipping a heart with the bit set means a ruined heart stores nothing — built as `GoldStore.Capacity` returning 0 for a ruined owner. `win-and-defeat.md` §4 | Closed |
| Q264 | **Is DK2's player debug dumper complete, and are its names load-bearing?** | **ANSWERED, and it is one of eleven.** 1,144 calls to the log `printf` cluster into eleven dumpers; ten name an object's fields and one is a profiler. All ten are transcribed — see `debug-dumpers.md` for the index, the method and the cautions. Together they name ~800 fields across creature, player, room, computer keeper, placed object, world, creature job/needs, steering body, task pool and level statistics | Closed. The names are a map, not a mechanism: each one still needs its writer read before any behaviour is built on it |
| Q265 | **Are `ResearchCompleted` and `ResearchPoint` the way round we assume?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-04): yes.** Research_AddPoints 0x004C2DC0 accumulates points in +0x20F and zeroes it on payment; +0x213 is the 1-based entry it passes to 0x004C30F0, 0x005070A0 and 0x00507040(+0x20F, +0x213), written by 0x004C3000. The dumper's names are DK2's labels; the completed count is +0x17B. `player-object.md` | — |
| Q266 | **Which keepers do the globals `[0x006F2548]` and `[0x006F254C]` name?** | **ANSWERED: neutral and hero** (ADR-0142). `0x006F254C` was already ours from the party counter and the Good-player spawn path. `0x006F2548` falls out of the prison drain, which exempts a creature whose owner is that id standing in a room of the same owner — an exemption that only makes sense for the player an unclaimed room belongs to, and the corpus places `NEUTRAL_CREATURE` records on prison tiles. Flame's map agrees; cited, not adopted | Closed |
| Q267 | **Which scoring system does a shipped level use — the hardcoded weights or the `MpdScore*` variables?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-04): split by mode.** MpdScore vars are read only at their events, in mode [0x0075AD4B]==4 for a keeper, and added to LevelScore (slot +0x344). The 0x0069F300 table is read only at level end (0x004BF211), in modes 1 and 4; +0x152 is unweighted, the 500 unused. So mode 4 runs both, the campaign the table (mode 1 = campaign is STRONGLY INFERRED). `player-object.md` | Owner: is mode 4 My Pet Dungeon and mode 1 the campaign? (STRONGLY INFERRED) |
| Q268 | **Does a held creature remain in its keeper's owned list?** | **STRONGLY INFERRED: yes.** Ownership insertion/unlink and hand pickup are audited in `creature-ownership.md`. Modern hand pickup also retains the creature and ownership order; the previous claim that it removes the creature was stale. | Binary question answered; a runtime type-19 comparison while holding creatures remains an optional cross-check. |
| Q269 | **What is `creature+0x28A`, the three-valued field conditions 20 and 21 partition on?** | **ANSWERED (binary and Text.str, 2026-09-30; writer 2026-10-05): the mood class, 0 Happy, 1 Unhappy, 2 Angry.** The tooltip `0x0048D880` (Text.str 2340/2342/2344) and `Panel_CreatureMatchesFilter` `0x00418A90` read it. Type 20 counts happy non-workers, type 21 angry ones. Built as ADR-0372 | — |
| Q270 | **What does room `TotalCapacity` count?** | **ANSWERED, STRONGLY INFERRED** (`auto-placed-furniture.md`); **the Training Room CONFIRMED (owner, 2026-09-25):** 3x3 on open ground shows 2, and against reinforced walls dummy + targets + 1. The ids are `RoomDef.ObjectIds`. Library, Training, Workshop and Casino use matching auto furniture + 1 when nonzero; Torture uses the raw count. Floor, wall and Temple passes are built | The wall-grid bit is cached `Solid`; only construction-order parity can still change placement. `UsedCapacity` at `room+0x6B` is a separate unread quantity |
| Q271 | **Are a player's `TagId` (`+0x04`) and `Number` (`+0x10FC`) ever different?** | **ANSWERED for level load (binary, 2026-10-04): equal.** Number has one writer, Players_Create 0x004C1E20 (index+1); TagId is the thing id from 0x00507850. GameAction_LoadLevel resets that cursor to 1 (0x00513994) just before Players_Create (0x00513A4B), so player i gets i+1 (slots 1-7 empty: STRONGLY INFERRED). | Q272 | **Where does the tick offset between our run and a capture come from?** | **ANSWERED: it is level-load jitter, and there is nothing to reproduce.** The "six ticks" was one sample. Across all six captures the tick DK2 stamps into timer 15 — its first trigger evaluation — is **4, 6, 7, 8, 10 and 11**, so the origin varies with the load and is not a rule. A comparison must therefore align on the first evaluation, which a timer's stored stamp gives exactly, never on absolute ticks. Aligned that way, level 1 idle leaves **+1 tick** on both sampled flags 568 apart | Closed for the origin. The residual +1 is Q273 |
| Q273 | **Is the one-tick residual ours or the probe's?** | **ANSWERED — the probe's (owner capture, 2026-09-10, `level20-idle-002`).** Level 20's timer 1 restamps inside an 11-minute idle window, and a timer slot holds the tick the game recorded when it acted. DK2 stored **1724**; we store 1724; the probe first *saw* it at **1725**. One event dates the lag against the game's own record. Applied to three events it was not fitted to — Level 20 `f6` 2405, level 1 `f1` 40 and `f34` 608 — all three land exactly on ours. **No residual remains in the simulation** | Closed. ## Hero-party and effect-generator questions raised by ADR-0108

| Q | Question | What is known | How to settle it |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q254 | **How and when does a hero party spawn?** | **ANSWERED (binary 2026-09-24; `hero-parties-and-effect-generators.md`) and built (ADR-0197).** Action 14's handler `0x00519F90` finds the action point's centre tile, clears it, arms the party subtree and makes one creature per occupied slot: type from the slot (or Dwarf + `MpdRandomInvader` draws for a non-zero `PartyType`), gold and health as percentages of the level's maxima, `Level−1` level-ups plus the party's spawn count, the job triple, flags, trigger root, event 3; then the party walks at its slowest member's pace with followers linked to the leader. Hero gates add effects and state 269 | Q427 holds what the build leaves out |
| Q255 | **What does an effect generator do at runtime?** | **PARTLY ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-05).** `0x004B1B60`: when active and due, `rand(4)` picks an effect slot from the simulation seed (an empty slot redraws next turn), then a random point in its Width × Height tiles and an angle; due = turn + Frequency × var19. Every level also gets a map-wide generator (effect 90, Frequency 1; `0x0050C265`), which makes the 2,982 line-173 draws in DK2's Level 1 census. Ours draws for neither; deferred, because the rate rests on `+0x20C` and DK2 draws about 14 a tick against ours under one, mostly cosmetic, so this site alone would not align the streams. `hero-parties-and-effect-generators.md` | What world slot `+0x20C` tests; what effect 90 is |
| Q256 | **Should party state and `TriggerTree.Parties` eventually have one owner?** | **RESOLVED (ADR-0197).** The world's `HeroPartyState` is the record the spawn reads and the one that carries `SpawnCount`; `TriggerTree.Parties` keeps only the subtree ids it disables at load, which is all the tree ever needed | — |
| Q257 | **Does DK2 transform `HeroPartyThing.Unknown_0023` or `Unknown_0027`?** | **Narrowed (2026-09-24).** The runtime party record keeps an 8-byte header (trigger id first) plus sixteen verbatim members, and the spawn handler reads only the trigger id, so neither field is consumed at spawn. Whether the loader keeps them at all is UNKNOWN | Read the party loader's header copy; until then storing them verbatim loses nothing |
| Q258 | **Does DK2 transform any nested `GoodCreatureThing` field when making a party member?** | **ANSWERED (binary + corpus 2026-09-24).** No: the member is copied verbatim and consumed field by field (`hero-parties-and-effect-generators.md`). The two-byte tail is `WanderRadius`, `PartyId`, copied to creature `+0x418`/`+0x417` — named so in the model since ADR-0197; all 947 shipped members carry their party's id and a zero radius. `GoldHeld` and `InitialHealth` are percentages; `Flags` bit 4 is never copied; `Objective` is a `JobType` | — |
| Q259 | **What are the effect generator's unknown fields and how are its effect-id list and frequency consumed?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-05).** The loader (`0x00521380`) reads `+0x10` Width and `+0x12` Height (spawn rectangle, tiles), four effect ids (picked uniformly), `+0x1C` Frequency (seconds) and `+0x1D` Id. `+0x08`, `+0x0C` and the six bytes at `+0x1E` are never read; ours no longer hashes them (hash 187). `hero-parties-and-effect-generators.md` | — |
| Q278 | **Does `player+0xEE6` count slaps given or received?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-04): received, by the slapped thing's owner.** All four writers add 1 to [thing+0x2A]+0xEE6: Hand_SlapCreature 0x0048B8A0 (not its slapper argument; states 0xD1/0xFF count and return early), 0x004A7E70 (trap), Object_ReactToSlap 0x0049C2A0, and 0x004A8E70 per trap it triggers. Built for creature slaps as ADR-0373, with the arm's no-count cases. `player-object.md` | Owner: can a keeper slap a rival's thing? If not, given = received |
| Q279 | **Possessed follower count and condition73** | **ANSWERED name, UNBUILT count.** The dumper names+0x488 `PossessedNumberOfFollowers`; see `creature-object.md`. Possession entry/exit reset it in the binary, but Stage7a does not model followers. Condition73 still declines its nonzero-pointer arm rather than inventing a count (ADR-0121). | Build count updates with the follower-control mechanic; possession lifecycle alone does not close this consumer. |
| Q280 | **What are `thing+0x3E6` bit 7 and `thing+0x146` bit 3, which area type 70 requires?** | **ANSWERED; creature branch BUILT (ADR-0124).** Both are `Possessed`, named independently by DK2's creature/object debug dumpers. Type 70 asks whether a possessed matching thing exists in the area. All six shipped records select creatures, so building that branch removes six stalls/eight gates. The unused object branch still declines because object possession is not state here | Add the object branch with the possession mechanism that can set and identify a possessed object; do not infer it from `InHand` |
| Q281 | **What is trap `thing+0x10C` bit 5 (`0x20`), used by condition 28?** | **ANSWERED AND BUILT (`doors-and-traps.md`, ADR-0141): `UnderConstruction`, STRONGLY INFERRED.** The constructor sets it, a zero authored construction marker leaves it set, completion clears it, save retains it, and firing/update bypass it. Condition 28 now counts it; 0 of 959 shipped authored traps start set | Closed for construction. Trap placement and firing remain Q249 |
| Q284 | **What is the door runtime dword at `thing+0x109`, on which condition 27 partitions its count?** | **ANSWERED AND BUILT (`doors-and-traps.md`, ADR-0141): `UnderConstruction`, STRONGLY INFERRED.** Constructor and unfinished authored doors set it; completion clears it; save retains it; construction bypasses ordinary update and removes the door's navigation contribution. Condition 27 now counts it; 3 of 940 shipped doors start set | Closed for construction. Door open/lock/navigation behavior remains Q249 |
| Q282 | **Why do six of the Mentor's 33 `silly` lines have no code path, and does the idle taunt fire at 160 s?** | **HYPOTHESIS.** Type 89 is the taunt pool and covers 27 of 33; ids 1166, 1169, 1170, 1172, 1185 and 1197 are referenced nowhere in `DKII.EXE` yet all six ship complete, distinct audio. The 160 s taunt and 180 s camera wander are read from two immediates and never timed. STRONGLY INFERRED; the owner has heard ids 1171 and 1198 (2026-09-10), which corrected the encoding bias to 10000. Evidence: `mentor-speech-and-notifications.md` | Nothing in `src/`. Gates whether restoring the six needs an ADR |
| Q283 | **When can condition type 64 be implemented?** | **ANSWERED AND BUILT (ADR-0122, ADR-0248, 2026-09-28).** Occupied templates minus active, non-dying creatures of any owner with that `PartyId`, through the arm's own comparison table at `0x00518AB0` (35 records ask `>=`, two `==`). The decline's blocker expired with ADR-0197 unnoticed. A Level 2 playback confirmed a knocked-out member counts as missing at once | Closed |

| Q285 | **What reaches the 513 shipped trigger records that no root of any class reaches?** | **PARTLY ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-04).** Objects are a sixth root class: the dumper 0x0049D310 shows TriggerRoot +0x144; when a neutral object changes owner, 0x0049C850 fires the event filter 0x004A3F50(18), which walks children with the world dispatchers. `kwd unreached` has no object roots. `trigger-walkers.md` | The writer of object +0x144 is not found; count shipped object TriggerIds and test whether the 513 sit under them |

Kinds 1 and 2 are dig-rock and dig-gold, CONFIRMED** (owner, 2026-09-10): four marks placed as two rock and two gold arrived as exactly two of each. Kinds **3, 6 and 14** were seen live and are stages of work rather than marks — a tile moves through them as an imp takes it up. The adder's 27 call sites also push 7, 8, 9, 10, 12, 13 and **16**, which the coordinate lookup, walking only 1…15, never checks | Sample with one activity at a time and watch which list it lands in; the entry's own `Type` and `Data` are now recorded alongside. `player-task-pool.md` |

| Q289 | **Do a room's `FirstSlab` and `LastSlab` bound it?** | **ANSWERED: they are the ends of a list, and bound the room once ordered** (run 10, 2026-09-10). The settled lair runs (8,30) to (10,26), descending in y, so a reader assuming a minimum and a maximum computes an empty box. Ordered, every settled room's span encloses exactly its slab count — gate 2×2=4, portal 3×3=9, heart 5×5=25, lair 3×5=15 — which **confirms `Size` is the slab count**. During construction they disagree (Q291) | Closed. `room-list.md`, ADR-0130 |
| Q291 | **Why does a room's `Size` run ahead of what `FirstSlab`/`LastSlab` enclose while it is being built?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-04).** The question misread the fields. They are the head and tail of the slab chain, not bounds. `Room_AddTile` `0x004EC650` links the tile after the tail, sets LastSlab and increments the word `Size` in consecutive instructions (`0x004EC791`–`0x004EC7A0`); `Room_Absorb` moves both together. That run 10's rows are a chain's ends during a build is STRONGLY INFERRED. `room-list.md` | none |
| Q292 | **How long does an imp take to claim one floor tile?** | **ANSWERED (run 15, 2026-09-10), BUILT (ADR-0133): five applications of 164.** The health trace reads 1, 165, 329, 493, 657, then Claimed Path. `ClaimTileHealth` 825 in 4096ths of Efficiency, shifted down 14, is 164 and not the 165 `tabled/5` gave — and 659 ÷ 164 crosses four where 659 ÷ 165 does not. Ours was four. The 9–22 tick task lengths that raised this were the pool bracketing the work, not the work (Q296) | — |
| Q293 | **What separates a finished dig from a cancelled one in the task pool?** | **ANSWERED: the claim that follows** (run 11, 2026-09-10, labelled play). Both remove the task, and the **Worker count does not separate them** — the owner cancelled a mark with three imps already digging, the same count a completion shows, which disproved the column added that morning for the job. A dug tile is claimed (kind 3) and a cancelled one stays rock, so a removal is a completion exactly when a claim follows before the tile is marked again | Closed. One wrinkle: a tile left and returned seven ticks later under a new task id — DK2 re-allocating, not the player. `player-task-pool.md`, ADR-0131 |
| Q294 | **How long does an imp take to reinforce one wall?** | **MEASURED at 61 ticks minimum** (run 11, 2026-09-10): pool kind 6 is reinforcing — every one sits on a wall adjacent to a tile just claimed, two queued the tick the claim finished, and the dig adder consults kind 6 at the same coordinates before adding (`0x00507C7B`). Four ran 61, 61, 70 and 76 ticks, each starting with its worker already assigned. Ours: the cadence was rebuilt from the Level 1 and 2 captures (ADR-0280, `mining-cadence.md` §5: first application three ticks after arrival, the second 3 later for wall work, then one a tick), not re-measured against the 61 | Measure ours on run 11's setup; is the spread above 61 a second imp's efficiency or an interruption? `player-task-pool.md`, `EXPERIMENTS.md` |
| Q295 | **Does a wall need claimed floor beside it to be reinforceable?** | **YES — CONFIRMED** (owner, 2026-09-10): *"as soon as the floor tile is claimed walls are eligible to be reinforced"*, and every kind-6 task in run 11 sits on a wall touching a tile just claimed. **BUILT as a guard, and it changes nothing today**: a wall is only ever reached from an orthogonally adjacent tile a worker stands on, so either that tile is the keeper's floor and the guard passes, or it is not and claiming outranks reinforcing. The determinism pin did not move, which is the tell | Watch it when anything lets a worker stand on ground it cannot claim — a bridge over water, a rival's floor. `player-task-pool.md`, ADR-0132 |
| Q296 | **Why is a work task in the pool longer-lived than the work?** | **ANSWERED (run 15): it brackets the work with an approach.** A claim task enters the pool about 5 ticks before the tile's health first moves and leaves about 2 after; reinforcing is 55 applications over 56 ticks inside a 61-tick task. So the spread in task lengths is walk time and the work itself is fixed | **Not the replay's residual**, which is now measured: our approach to the *first* marked tile matches DK2 tick for tick, and the lead is a fourth imp we have and DK2 did not (Q298, `EXPERIMENTS.md`) |
| Q297 | **Why does digging a Reinforced Wall reach Dirt Path, not the Rock its `DestroyedTypeTerrainId` names?** | **ANSWERED, BUILT (ADR-0134).** The breaking swing creates Rock at 165; the marked dig task survives and each later application selects its rate again from current owner/type (`0x00496E75`, `0x00496FE3`, `0x00497090`, call `0x0049713D`). A last-in-tick break leaves Rock visible; a following rock-rate application can remove it before sampling. Ours retains Q20's two-swing correction, so two queued orders remove 100 then 65. No overflow or chained destroy. STRONGLY INFERRED mechanism, CONFIRMED timelines. `runtime-map.md` | — |
| Q298 | **Why did a level-1 keeper work with three imps when it had four?** | **ANSWERED (owner, 2026-09-10): it had four.** So the fourth is real and never swung: run 15 shows no fourth worker in 117 working ticks, and the whole 60-second task pool held only the six tiles of the experiment, so it had **no task at all** while two marked tiles waited 13+ ticks. Ours sends its fourth to the next mark, and that is the entire 18–37 tick lead the replay shows | The count is settled; what the fourth imp was doing is not — Q299. `EXPERIMENTS.md` |
| Q299 | **What does a DK2 imp do when the job it wants is already at its worker cap?** | **ANSWERED (binary 2026-09-30), BUILT (ADR-0289; `imp-task-stealing.md` §1).** There is no cap to wait at: a handler that finds a held slot takes it when it is strictly nearer than the holder by its own measure (the dig handler allows `0x32` slack), sets the holder to state 10 with its job block kept, and the loser reselects — reselection capped at four imps a keeper a tick; past the cap the loser rests in state 12. `TaskStealing` is the shared holder test (Q155). Run 15 (three imps on the first mark, two marks idle, the fourth waiting) has not been re-run against it | Re-run run 15's setup in the replay rig before treating the divergence as live. `EXPERIMENTS.md`, `imp-task-priority.md` |
| Q300 | **What are the 32 state-category records' own field values?** | **ANSWERED, BUILT (2026-09-10; corrected 2026-09-24, ADR-0195).** The fill run writes 511 dwords; record 0's bit is the file-backed `0x00000001`. A whole-program operand scan finds no later direct, indexed or block write. `+0x38` is identity; `+0x34` is an entry mask and zero means unrestricted (`state-categories.md`) | All 78,680 ordered pairs resolve. Residual: `+0x3c` and the still-unnamed categories |
| Q301 | **What does action type 22 do beyond writing the tile?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-10-04).** It first sells a room tile on the spot through its owner, no refund (0x004C5DB0(x,y,0)). Plain terrain: Tile_SetTerrain with the player's TagId (non-ownable goes neutral). Room terrain: room via 0x0050B550; a water/lava room lays world+0x9BAE/+0x9BAF first, neutral; then Player_BuildRoomTile free, no placement test. Both raise effect 0x146. Built as ADR-0363 (Siege and Secret3 bridges now build); effect 0x146 not modelled. `trigger-action-arms.md` | — |
