| Q341 | **What is DK2's route search over the navigation mesh?** | **ANSWERED (binary + owner, 2026-09-16), BUILT and wired** (`MeshRouter`, ADR-0157 phase 1; `navigation-mesh-and-search.md`). **Greedy best-first**: a 1024-entry min-heap over triangles keyed on the nearest vertex's squared distance to the goal, shifted by a 1–4 transition cost — no accumulated distance anywhere. Run from both ends, each funnelled, the shorter kept (`Bak reverse choice`). Owner-CONFIRMED routes are not tile-constrained. The triangulation's internals are split off as Q347 | Not Dijkstra, not A*: DK2's router is not optimal, and a faithful replacement must not be either |
| Q342 | **What reduction is `Route2path`?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-16, second reader; spot-verified), BUILT and wired** (`Funnel`, ADR-0157 phase 1; `navigation-and-steering.md` §2.1). The standard **funnel**: `0x004CEF40` walks the triangle route, takes each shared edge as a gate from `0x004CCD50`, and emits a gate endpoint whenever the corridor bounds cross. The mover then takes **gate midpoints** — `0x004D5100` asks with `0x2000` over `0x4000` — up to five at a time, into `subpath_buffer`, with no tile rounding anywhere | This is why the owner's 10-by-3 walk is one straight line: with no turn needed, the path is the destination alone. Our A* cannot produce that route |
| Q343 | **How are the eight steering behaviours combined, and with what weights?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-16), BUILT and wired** (`SteeringIntegrator`, ADR-0157 phase 2; `navigation-and-steering.md` §§3.3–3.4). A **weighted sum of individually clamped forces**, then Euler integration: clamp each force to `max_force`, scale by weight, sum, clamp the sum again, divide by a mass of 1, add to velocity, clamp that to `max_speed`. Weights, addresses and the three hard-coded scalars are in §§3.3–3.4 | Position advances by the velocity each tick and snaps onto the final waypoint when the step would overshoot (§3.4), so the steering layer is read end to end. `avoid_obstacle` is implemented and never registered |
| Q344 | **What is the `grtc:` stage?** | **ANSWERED (binary, two readers, 2026-09-17), BUILT and wired** (`GateWalk`, ADR-0157; `navigation-and-steering.md` §2.1). `0x004CF490` re-walks the route's gates after the funnel: a gate is kept unless all four sight-lines from the last kept gate's endpoints to the next gate's endpoints pass through it; the endpoint nearer the funnel polyline is selected; gates of sixteen tiles or more are capped around the polyline's crossing (`0x004CFC50`, `0x004CFDA0`, `0x004CFFD0`). The cap is the wrapper's literal `0x1000`, not the mover's `0x2000`, which is the point selector's midpoint fraction | Every route point is the midpoint of a kept gate, then the destination; a straight route keeps none. UNKNOWN whether any shipped map has a sixteen-tile gate |
| Q345 | **Do creatures collide with each other, or share a position?** | **PARTLY BUILT (2026-10-12).** Owner observes pushing. Original soft separation uses overflowing arithmetic and exempts zero distance; it guarantees no rigid clearance. Route-less fight-state contact now integrates (ADR-0447). A controlled Level8 bridge probe confirms motion but retains some overlap. The remembered-entity supplier remains absent: its nonzero writer is UNKNOWN, and substituting the current enemy is unsupported. See [combat-spacing.md](combat-spacing.md) and navigation-and-steering.md §3.2. | Capture original and modern bridge fights from separated starts; compare body distances, flags and poses. Trace indirect activation of `+0x537/+0x539` before wiring the remembered term. |
| Q346 | **Why does Separation's saturation arm push less hard than the curve it replaces?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-16) — it does not** (`navigation-and-steering.md` §3.3). Seen from the consumer, every behaviour's force is truncated to `max_force` by `0x004D4F60` *before* it is weighted, so the curve arm's very large magnitude is clamped to exactly what the saturation arm returns directly. The discontinuity is in the magnitude and never reaches the force | Asked because an operation-for-operation transcription still looked incoherent. It was answered by reading the caller, not by re-reading the callee. `TheSaturationArmCanUndercutTheCurveItReplaces` still pins the magnitudes |
| Q347 | **How does DK2 triangulate the navigation-cache bytes into the mesh?** | **ANSWERED at the algorithm level (binary, 2026-09-16), BUILT and wired** (`NavMesh`, ADR-0157 phase 1; `navigation-mesh-and-search.md` §2.1). Reset to two triangles over the map; decompose the rebuild rectangle into **maximal uniform rectangles** of one nav byte; insert each as four corner points and four **constraint segments** (edge insertion by orientation-guided flipping, recursing at vertices); stamp the interior; merge same-byte neighbours. **No in-circle test anywhere** — constrained, not Delaunay | Where gates fall is now read: a tile edge between two different nav bytes is a constraint, a run of one byte never is. The flag bits and region ids are Q348 |
| Q348 | **What are a triangle's six edge-flag bits and its ten-bit id?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-16), NOT BUILT** (`navigation-mesh-and-search.md` §2.2). `+0xE` low bits: a **2-bit width class per edge** (tiles spanned, clamped 1–3), read by the search through per-slot masks against a mover-class table — a **clearance mechanism the shipped game never engages**, the class byte being always 0. `+0xE` high bits: a **BFS region id** nothing outside the builder reads. `+0xD` bits 3–5: the edge is a **constraint**, which the flip refuses to cross | A faithful router needs the constraint bits and may ignore classes and regions; neither changes a shipped route. `+0xD` bits 0–2 stay unread and nothing in routing depends on them |
| Q349 | **What do `Avoid_Walls` and `Follow_Path` compute, and how does the integrator clip velocity against solid obstacles?** | **ANSWERED (binary 2026-09-17, codex as second reader; `steering-behaviours.md` §2–§7), BUILT and wired.** `Follow_Path`: progress and a 15-tick stuck history; imp and creature arms; **the creature turns before it moves** at the definition's `+0x6A0` rate and brakes meanwhile; a crowd probe (sum of creature `+0x200` over a 0.95-tile square) collapses the seek near an occupied waypoint. Integrator: the slide's projection and tangent sign, the five-arm wall clamp, the ray-circle test with its non-textbook discriminant | Closed: the crowd word is `((definition +0x63A) >> 1) << 4`, a footprint radius from KWD `0xEA0`; nothing left |
| Q350 | **Which route option word does each creature state pass, and what does it do?** | **ANSWERED (binary, 2026-09-17), BUILT** (`MoveOptions`; steering-behaviours.md §7.6). `0x004D0F60` unpacks it: bits 1, 2 and 7 are door rules for the cost function, bits 3–6 become `path_flags` (3 → tight arrival, 4 → no final approach, 5 or 6 → snap and separate until arrival). 118 of 134 sites push a literal: `0x80` (loose, respect doors) is the norm; `0xE8` and the `0x68` family (exact spot) go with bed, chicken, wages, gambling and captives; imps en route pass 0. So bit 3 never travels alone and the earlier default of 8 was wrong | Sixteen sites push a register and are unread, among them the main update's own request at `0x00489E17` and states 1, 86, 100 and 186 |
| Q351 | **Why do our corridors hold one to three more triangles than DK2's?** | **ANSWERED (2026-09-18; navigation-captures.md).** Three causes, all built. ` route=` is triangles - 1, so the systematic +1 was the count convention. A whole-map rebuild resets to a -256..1280 canvas, stamps the map as one rectangle of byte 0, then **skips every scanned rectangle whose byte is 0** (`0x004CEB56`), and removes no vertex - that pass is gated on the partial-rebuild flag. Coarsening (`0x004CC650`, after each rectangle) flips an edge when both triangles share a byte, the quad is convex and the other diagonal is shorter, never entering rock. Level 10: our points are DK2's exactly, 1,417 of 1,417; 2,612 of 2,765 triangles identical; corridors exact in 84 % against 33 % | - |
| Q352 | **Does DK2 route onto the Heart's inner 3×3?** | **ANSWERED (captured cache, 2026-09-17): yes — the whole heart is floor to the router.** DK2's navigation cache, dumped from a running Level 7, holds 0 on all 25 tiles of both hearts, centre included; 411 logged requests start or end on the platform. `WorldSteering.ByteOf` now follows the cache and the router walks the platform; the path grid and the Hand keep Q88's block. What the owner saw creatures avoid — a circle about a tile wide around the centre, marked on a screenshot the same day — is the heart's obstacle object, Q355 | The same dump put byte 4 on exactly the two portal centres and nowhere else: bit 2 on a plain tile is the portal hole, entered only with option bit 2 |
| Q353 | **What is left of DK2's door model after ADR-0158?** | **ANSWERED (binary 2026-09-18; doors-and-traps.md), and built (ADR-0159).** The three tiles are the door's and its two passage neighbours, chosen by the world predicate `+0x208`. Creature flag `+0x3E6` bit 21 is `CreatureDying`. Definition byte `+0x8B` is the record's flags at 0x228: bit 5 Secret, bit 6 both barricades (a permanent lock), bit 7 hero doors. `0x004A6800` has three callers - loader (3), the lock command (a toggle, 3 or 0), an effect handler (0; the finale walk state 234, `0x00474F80`) - so modes 1 and 2 never occur; the pick duration is `DoorPickedTimeSeconds`. Alliances are eight symmetric bytes at `player+0x1AE`; the pick timer counts down ahead of the lock mode | — |
| Q354 | **Where does DK2 put a route endpoint that lies exactly on a tile corner?** | **ANSWERED (binary 2026-09-18; navigation-mesh-and-search.md §2.3), BUILT.** `0x004C9DF0` locates both endpoints: a walk to a triangle whose closed boundary holds the point, then the tie is broken toward the far point (0x10100, 0x10100). A vertex point takes the fan triangle around it whose wedge holds that point (`0x004C9540`, rotating across the leaving edge); an edge point takes the neighbour when the far point is outside. So a corner shared with a solid tile resolves to its south-east tile. `NavMesh.LocateEndpoint`; the replay's 93 corner disagreements vanish and 21,288 of 21,298 requests agree | The ten left need the requester's own lava and portal-hole flags |
| Q355 | **How does the Heart keep creatures off its platform, when its tiles are floor to the router?** | **ANSWERED (binary + Level 7 and 10 captures, 2026-09-18; structure-blocks.md §5.3), BUILT.** The gated enumerator `0x004D12B0` skips object 100 in its tile walk and, when the query touches heart terrain, forwards the `Dungeon Heart Obstacle` on the room's stored centre once: size width/2 = 1.5 tiles, flag bits 0 and 1. The centre tile also holds the visible `Dungeon Heart` (13) and `Arches` (86), plain shoves of 0.5 and 0.3 tiles: the last half tile creatures never enter. Captured imps ride the 1.5 ring; one stalled at the heart's wall then crossed the platform at full speed, so escape mode drops the contact in DK2 as in ours | — |
| Q356 | **Can DK2's own packet recorder give us a repeatable run and the player's own inputs?** | **ANSWERED (2026-09-18; navigation-captures.md).** Yes. `-LEVEL <lvl> -PSAVE <name>` records to `PacketRecord\<name>.sav`; `-LEVEL <lvl> -PLOAD <name> 0` replays, and `-LEVEL` is required on both. A turn is counter, RNG seed, state checksum, a 4-byte header, N 18-byte commands and a 13-byte camera; a five-minute session replayed with no seed divergence in 2,924 turns and every `MESSAGE.LOG` counter identical. **Action 40 tags for digging, 41 untags**, and recordings of known actions in a known order named fourteen more (table in navigation-captures.md), cross-checked against the room, door and spell tables via the `Availability` enum the selection command carries | 88, 93 and 106 unnamed |
| Q357 | **Why did our waypoint count differ from DK2's on two dozen replayed routing requests?** | **ANSWERED (2026-09-18; navigation-captures.md).** Not a door's state: the replay scored every request against a profile that respects doors, and most of this traffic does not. Imps en route, attacking and patrol push `MoveOptions.Plain`, no door rule at all, and 65,583 of the session's 98,313 navigation calls are the imp pass. Clearing that bit takes the disagreements from 24 to 2, and the respecting profile explains one of those. The earlier "one door tile was wrong, 23 times in 23" is withdrawn: it reproduces 1 of 22 under its own change placement, and 0 of 3,254 controls reproduce both numbers | The 1 of 20,926 no profile reproduces: F=1765, DK2 way=6 against our 5 |

A win makes every keeper that is not a mutual ally lose; a loss with the time limit unexpired makes the last remaining keeper win when alone, or when every other remaining keeper is its mutual ally, its playing allies with it. Invalid target, changed selection or route failure ends the route and resumes state 199. `+0xDF/+0xEB` are next state 199, not options; `+0xF5` is the refresh countdown. It remains attackable but category 22 suppresses `SelectAVictim`. `NeedBodyForFightIdle` routes to the nearest eligible neutral dead body within squared range `0x2000`, validates it in state 259, and performs the same pause there without consuming the body. Job 16 sets it before either branch (`0x004751AF`), so state 271 never re-draws and the deadline is never reloaded; the bit also stops need anger (`0x00478D4F`) and eat, sleep and pay attempts (`0x00477BDA`). (b) `0x00491AE0` gates a fresh follow of the possessed creature (name LIKELY): not following (`+0x482`), not Horny, group not 1, `+0x3CB` zero, not `RebelActive`, mood class Happy; callers `0x00515D50` and `0x00461980`. The two **route helpers** do: `0x00471890(point, resume, options)` enters state 6 and `0x00471A70(room, resume, options)` enters state 7, each writing `+0xDF` immediately after. The resume is the travel handler's own arrival branch - stop, `SetState(+0xDF)`, then `+0xDF = 1` whether or not the move was allowed. No hook, no polling. The slot is never unset during a walk, so ADR-0174's fallback is unreachable in the original | Closed. The thirteen travelling activities can now retire; the gate's empty-slot value wants to become 1, not -1 |

Idle imps pausing as if waiting (2026-09-24) **do not bear on 88**: state 12 plays action 43 too (`imp-idle-and-wandering.md` §5) | A replay capture logging every creature's state id, which would also catch a computed call site or a saved state; its result decides whether 88 is built |

## Numbering

New questions take the number after the highest in this file. Rows are never renumbered,
reordered or deleted, so file order is not numeric order: Q46–Q48 sit among Q22–Q31,
Q60–Q61 and Q49–Q51 precede Q52, Q55x precedes Q55, Q135 precedes Q134, Q125 and Q120–Q123
follow Q134, Q166 follows Q179, Q187 and Q189 precede Q184, Q214–Q219 follow Q209, and
**Q210–Q213 sit after Q234** in the Hero Portal section above, with Q235–Q242 after them,
and **Q243–Q245 were reserved for a concurrent branch that needed none of them**;
they are free. Q249–Q253 belong to ADR-0106; Q254–Q259 belong to ADR-0108.
**Q318–Q321 were the reserved graveyard block** (ADR-0151, merged 2026-09-16); **Q322–Q324 were reserved with them and never used, and are free** — leave them, a spent reservation reused reads as a mistake. **Q330–Q334 were the reserved creature-walker block** (ADR-0152, merged 2026-09-16) and only Q330 was spent; treat Q331–Q334 the same way as Q322–Q324. Take Q358 next.
**Q39 appears twice** and both rows are kept; Q37a/Q37b, Q55x and Q118c are separate
suffixed ids, not duplicates. **Q121 was never allocated** and must not be reused.

| Q568 | **Is a converted KMF the DK2 model or its mirror image?** | **ANSWERED (owner's play, 2026-10-06): not mirrored.** In DK2 the Imp holds its pick in its right hand, as the converted Imp does in Blender; a mirror would put it in the left. `DK2.Convert` maps DK2 (x, y, z) to glTF (x, −z, y), a rotation, so DK2's model space reads right-handed. Consistent with the renders: the converted Knight, Black Knight, Dwarf, Goblin and Skeleton hold weapons in the right hand, the Skeleton its shield in the left. Unexplained and harmless: DK2's triangle order runs against its stored normals (98% of 1,990 GLBs' triangles agree only once reversed), so DK2's cull convention is the reverse of glTF's | — |

From a category 0 state such as 3 the move to 211 is refused by the transition table, so a creature idling in 3 does not cheer. | Done |

It keeps the lifetime it got at death, `DeadBodyDiesAfterSeconds` x 4 turns, counted down in a Graveyard as on the floor (`0x004B12D0`). The thing sweep's removal pass destroys it below 1 (`0x004B7830`, slot `+0x30` `0x004B16E0`), which buries it (`0x004B1070`). So a 3x3 Graveyard holds one corpse at a time: the owner confirms a second is left to rot, the Vampire comes only once enough have rotted there, and every floor slot shows a tombstone.
